- 2026-09-05 · **A connected MCP server is necessary, not sufficient**: the adapter's init check now also requires at least one `mcp__qwry__*` tool in `system/init.tools`; a connected-but-toolless child is killed before the model speaks. Belt for the gate above and for any future CLI change of the same shape.

### Agent A1 · W2b (taste, 2026-09-05; picture in ~/projects/qwry-agent-lab/docs/ask-sketch-v2.html)
- 2026-09-05 · **One right pane, two modes**: Inspector and Ask are modes of ONE card (`src/stores/sidePane.ts`: mode, open, width; it replaces the open/width halves of `inspector.ts` and `ask.ts`), never two cards fighting for the right side. The titlebar pair is a radio (lit icon = mode; Ask wears lucide `MessageSquare`, the sparkles go); ⌘J / ⌘I open the pane in that mode or switch it, and the chord of the showing mode closes it. Width is shared, floors per mode (Inspector 220, Ask 320), switching clamps. Supersedes the W2 480px width budget (the open effect AND `sideCardDragCap`), the second drag handle and the second persisted width: one pane needs no budget.
- 2026-09-05 · **Header diet**: the Ask header is `Ask` (text) · `Threads` · `New Thread` and nothing else (DESIGN rule 12, the Inspector's own header shape). Avatar, name, database, the READ-ONLY badge, the model pill and its tier leave it: the connection is the pane's binding (sidebar + placeholder + footer avatar), PROD stays the titlebar chip, and read-only is a promise the anatomy keeps (a SQL row on every answer), not a badge. Supersedes the W2 READ-ONLY-badge line and the header half of the per-block-provenance line; DESIGN rule 5's 14px avatar exception now names the footer alone.
- 2026-09-05 · **The composer is two rows and has no hint line**: textarea, then model pill (left) · accent send/stop at the 28px icon-button tier (right). The W2 hint line (`↩ ask · ⇧↩ newline · read-only · every answer shows its SQL · thinking · ⌘. cancels`) is deleted under DESIGN rule 11; chords live in the send button's tooltip and the Keyboard Shortcuts sheet, and the Stop face IS the cancel affordance. The configured empty state is the three starters docked above the composer and nothing else; the slogan sentence goes.
- 2026-09-05 · **The picker moves to the composer's control row** and opens UPWARD from the pill (AnchoredOverlay anchored to the pill's left edge, not the W2 right-edge drop): pill = short name + chevron, `small` alone badges the pill; rows = name · optional ctx · tier badge; groups = provider name (a local runtime appends its URL; a provider that cannot answer appends `· no key` / `· not running`; the W2 `· subscription` / `· key saved` / `· checking` qualifiers go, rule 11); footer `Manage Models…`. Nothing explains tiers anywhere: the tier bubbles (`role=tooltip` on hover / focus / arrow-hot), `TIER_SENTENCE` and the `mid?` face are removed and AGENT-UX §8's one-sentence-per-tier rule is repealed. Supersedes the W2 tier-bubble line and the `Manage in Settings › Models…` face.
- 2026-09-05 · **Answer text = the model's LAST text block**, one or two sentences: `src/agent/loop.ts` stops concatenating pre-tool narration across turns (`text += ev.text` ran over every turn, so "Now retrieving…" became the answer's prefix); the final turn's block is the answer and every earlier block is a trace row. Before display the SQL fence, the `Assumptions:` line and any markdown table are stripped (DESIGN rule 14: each has its own slot), bold and inline code render, headings / lists / tables / links never; the raw text stays in the trace untouched. The prompt asks for one or two sentences and ≤6-word assumption labels.
- 2026-09-05 · **Footer = avatar · turns · time · model · `Trace`** (`1 turn · 20.4 s · Sonnet 5`): the connection name is dropped (the avatar is the provenance mark), `How did it get this?` becomes `Trace`, and the line fits at 320 on one line (DESIGN rule 13). Units take a space (`20.4 s`, WRITING status register, `12 rows · 3.1 ms`); reloaded threads keep hiding zero turns and zero ms (LESSONS 9).
- 2026-09-05 · **Assumption chips wrap** (`min-height` 24px, `line-height` 1.35, `white-space: normal`): a long label grows the pill downward and the row onto further lines, never clips inside the pill or scrolls; the row leads with `Assumed`. Labels are capped at the model (≤6 words in the prompt), never at the pill.
- 2026-09-05 · **One ms formatter**: the Ask status line prints `run.ms` in the results pane's shape (`toFixed(1)` + ` ms`, `1861.9 ms`, ResultsPane's own literal), never the raw float (`1861.922375 ms`); the footer's seconds share the one-decimal, spaced-unit rule (`20.4 s`).
- 2026-09-05 · **A readOnly grid fills its slot**: when the columns' natural widths fit the slot the last column stretches to the right edge; otherwise the grid scrolls as everywhere else. At 320 the header cut `notification_count` mid-name at natural widths while empty space sat to its right.
- 2026-09-05 · **Pixel evidence comes from a fixture harness, not from AX-driving the app**: a vite-served route renders AskPanel with a canned thread per state (answer / empty / busy / picker / failure) at a given width and theme, and `scripts/ask-frames.ts` drives headless Chrome over it to write `<out>/<state>-<width>-<theme>.png` for 320 / 392 / 560 × dark / light. The W2 gotchas (Spaces, menu tracking loops, single-instance) made synthetic input lose eight events in one gate; the dev build remains the final eyeball, not the evidence.
- 2026-09-05 · **`.claude/skills/taste-gate` is the gate's procedure**: frames → rules 11–14 as yes/no per frame → FINDINGS with frame evidence and a pass / block verdict, S1 blocks and a block means a fixer round, never "awaiting taste pass". A Fable-class reviewer signs the verdict (restraint is judgment, not rule-matching). Law stays in DESIGN.md; the skill only says how to run it, so every session and workflow agent runs the same gate.
- 2026-09-05 · **DESIGN.md rules 11–14** (every string earns its pixels · a strip states one thing · floor first · data once) plus the rule 9 extension (frames at floor / default / max of a live state, both themes): W2 satisfied every rule its reviewers had and still shipped a hint line, a ten-item header, a single-width sketch and prose that repeated the grid. DESIGN was a consistency law and WRITING a register law; neither spoke of restraint, and "which rule made producing this okay?" had the answer "none forbade it".
- 2026-09-05 · **Both mode holders stay mounted**: the one right pane renders the Inspector holder and the Ask holder at once and the one off stage is `visibility: hidden` via `data-mode`, never unmounted, so a mode switch keeps the inspector's target and scroll and Ask's streamed thread alive; the collapsed pane hides both holders after `--dur-slow` (hidden controls leave the tab order, DESIGN rule 2, ROADMAP_log W2 gotcha). The App effect moves focus out of a hidden holder explicitly (to `.inspector-fixed`, or the composer via `requestFocus`), never trusting the engine to blur it.
- 2026-09-05 · **`qwry.sidePane` adopts `qwry.inspector` once**: a fresh sidePane store with nothing of its own reads the legacy inspector blob's open + width, so the maintainer's dragged inspector width survives the store move; its own key wins from then on. Ask defaulted closed and never owned the launch state, so its old key is not consulted. `useInspector.open/width` and `useAsk.open` are read-only mirrors of the pane for their existing readers (TraceDrawer, Inspector, JsonTree); the setters (`useAsk.setOpen/toggle/setWidth`, `useInspector.setWidth`, `ASK_W_*`) are gone.
- 2026-09-05 · **AskEvent `narration`**: a tool call closes the text block before it; the loop emits the block as `narration`, the store clears the answer slot, and `AskAnswer.text` plus the persisted `agent_turns.content` are the LAST block (live/reload parity, LESSONS 9: `openThread` re-derives SQL and assumptions from `content`, both of which live in the last block). Earlier blocks stay raw inside the turn's trace row; the trace keeps one `turn` row per qwry turn, so an ownsLoop run is `context · turn · tool… · verdict`, and splitting narration into its own rows is the TraceDrawer's later call. `runSmall` is untouched: the small tier has no tool calls and no block boundary.
- 2026-09-05 · **`src/lib/duration.ts` is the status register's one clock**: `msText` (`1861.9 ms`) and `secondsText` (`20.4 s`), extracted from ResultsPane byte-identically, so the Ask status line, the footer and the results pane move together; `footerStatus` lives in `src/agent/display.ts` (testable without the grid) and hides zero turns and zero ms.
- 2026-09-05 · **No rowless teaching group in the picker**: `groupsFrom` no longer emits `Anthropic · no key` when Anthropic is not the chosen provider. A heading with no rows teaches what `Manage Models…` directly beneath it already offers (DESIGN rule 11; the locked sketch's picker shows Claude Code and llama.cpp only). Supersedes the W2 picker-groups line's "ONE teaching group"; the chosen provider still shows `· no key` / `· not running` above the hand-off so the pill's choice is never orphaned. Group titles are the bare provider name except the three exceptions the sketch draws (`· 127.0.0.1:8080`, `· no key`, `· not running`); `· subscription`, `· key saved`, `· checking` and the `mid?` option face are gone.
- 2026-09-05 · **A failed statement is titled `query failed`**, never `stopped after N turns`: that sentence is the turn cap's own (AGENT-UX 7) and a SQL error wearing it reads as a turn budget, a false message (LESSONS 9). `FailureBlock` drops its `turns` prop; the turn-cap message carries the count itself.
- 2026-09-05 · **Disabled chips win over motion's inline opacity**: every `layoutId` element (the follow-up and starter chips) gets an inline `opacity: 1` from motion's shared-layout projection, which outranked `button:disabled`, so a disabled chip looked pressable (disconnected starters; every earlier exchange's follow-ups while a run is busy). `.chip:disabled { opacity: var(--o-disabled) !important }` in ask.css is the one sanctioned `!important` here, found by a CDP probe of the harness page, not by reading CSS (LESSONS 8).
- 2026-09-05 · **Frames are stills of the settled state, captured over CDP**: `scripts/ask-frames.ts` drives each headless Chrome through the DevTools protocol (navigate, poll the harness's `data-harness-ready` mark, a real 700 ms settle, `Page.captureScreenshot` at scale 2) with `--force-prefers-reduced-motion`, so every springs.ts preset is its instant variant and tokens.css collapses the CSS transitions. Chrome's own `--screenshot` was the flaky half: under `--virtual-time-budget` one busy frame in three shot before the thinking strip's entry spring had advanced, and with instant transitions the portaled popover's layer had not rastered (an empty popover box, a missing row). Motion is the dev build's eyeball, never a still's.
- 2026-09-05 · **The harness matrix is seven states** (`answer` `empty` `busy` `picker` `failure` `disconnected` `small`, 42 frames): `disconnected` and `small` are the composer's two exceptions the sketch does not draw (no session: textarea disabled, pill and starters dimmed; the one pill that wears a badge), each one prop away from the configured empty state. The fixture's trace mirrors the loop's shape (one `turn` row with the whole raw text, then the tools), carries a fence and an `Assumptions:` line the display strip must remove, and a third five-word assumption so the chip row wraps at the floor (DESIGN rule 9: real long content).
- 2026-09-05 · **Follow-up and starter chips wear `ArrowRight`, not the sketch's `?` glyph**: each label ends in a question mark, so a leading `?` doubles it; `→` is the house go-mark (ImportWizard's step arrow, WRITING's `Open Referenced orders →`) and names the action the chip performs when it morphs into the echo. The sketch's `.chip.q` glyph is redrawn to match.
- 2026-09-05 · **The send button disables until the draft is non-empty** (`disabled={!busy && !canSend}`, `canSend = connected && !busy && draft.trim() !== ""`, AskPanel.tsx): the locked sketch draws the accent button live over an empty composer, but a live-looking button that does nothing is a false affordance (LESSONS 9, DESIGN rule 2), so the `--o-disabled` face is the truthful one and the sketch's empty composer is the side to redraw. The Stop face is never dimmed while a run is busy.

The prompt asks for plain words ("say orders, not order_v2"), trends, joins and distributions, no generic row count, nothing outside the listed tables.
- 2026-09-06 · **`schemaHash` is FNV-1a (32 bits, eight hex digits) over the sorted `schema.table(col,col,…)` lines of the snapshot**: stable across table order and machines, changed by any table or column added or renamed, untouched by row estimates and comments (they are not shape). It lives in `src/agent/starterPool.ts` so the store, the hook and the harness share one function.
- 2026-09-06 · **The starter prompt's schema summary is the whole schema's largest askable tables** (plain and partitioned parents only; partitions and inheritance children, system schemas, the migration ledger and LEGACY twins are out, read from the snapshot's `parent_oid` because `buildMeta` keeps a child whose parent is a plain table): at most 25 tables by row estimate, `name(cols, … +N)  -- ~rows; comment` per table plus the FK edges among them, kept under 16k characters (~4k tokens) by dropping the smallest tables to eight, then halving the column cap from 40 to eight, then dropping tables to one. The excluded names ride along as `banned` and the parser drops any question that names one as a whole identifier, so `order_v2` never bans "orders".
- 2026-09-06 · **`parseStarters` reads a JSON array or one question per line** (bullets, numbering, quotes stripped), keeps only complete questions of at most `STARTER_WORD_CAP` (12) words ending in `?`, drops lead-in lines, the generic row count in every costume (`rows … each/every/per/all … table`, `table … row count`, `how many tables`), any banned identifier and any repeat (case- and punctuation-insensitive), and caps at twelve: the same rules starters.test.ts pins for the heuristic pool.
- 2026-09-06 · **`useStarterPools` (`qwry.starters`) persists `{ pools, cursors }` only**, sanitising at hydrate (well-formed pools, finite cursors, never a throw); `ensurePool` is a no-op when the connection's pool carries the current hash, fires at most one generation per connection at a time (a module-level `inFlight` set, not state), swallows every failure, and `generator` is its test seam in agent.ts's `runner` shape. The cursor steps by `CURSOR_STEP` (3) and wraps at `CURSOR_WRAP` (27 720, the lcm of 1..12), so `cursor % n` stays continuous across the wrap for every pool size a triple can be cut from. A schema change while a generation is in flight lands a pool keyed to the older hash and regenerates on the next empty-state mount, not at once; `generatedAt` is stored and unused.
- 2026-09-06 · **The triple a mount shows is fixed at that mount**: `useStarters` reads the cursor into a per-connection ref in its mount effect and advances the store once; a re-render (the generated pool landing, the thread list arriving) recomputes the three from the same start, so the swap on landing is the pool's, never a cursor jump, and StrictMode's second effect pass is guarded by the ref. The hook resolves `modelChoice(profileId)` itself (its signature is fixed at profileId, snapshot, asked) and calls `ensurePool` whenever profile, snapshot or choice change.

- 2026-09-06 · **A probe chip names its subject**: `chipLabel` for `probe` is `probe <noun>` through `probeNoun(sqls)` (src/agent/probeNoun.ts, a tokenizer not a parser): the first column the FIRST statement's SELECT list measures (`min(sent_at), max(sent_at)` → `sent_at`), then the WHERE column when the list measures only `count(*)` (`WHERE created_at < signup_at` → `created_at`), then GROUP BY, then the first table (`SELECT count(*) FROM payment` → `payment`), else the bare `probe`. ### Agent A1 · W5 (rich answer text, prompt v3 and the presentation score, 2026-09-06; picture: the "W5 · rich answer text" rows of ~/projects/qwry-agent-lab/docs/ask-sketch-v2.html)
- 2026-09-06 · **The answer slot is a block grammar, not a display strip** (`parseBlocks` in `src/agent/display.ts` replaces `renderInline`; `answerText` stays the plain projection loop.ts, the thread list and the eval read): Block = `p` · `lead` · `list` · `quote` · `code` · `table`; a SQL fence (`sql` / `postgres` / `postgresql` / `pgsql`, or a langless fence whose first word is a statement) never renders because the SQL row has it, the `Assumptions:` span strips as before, an image renders as nothing; pure, no React, 1000 parses of a 4k answer in 28.7 ms against a 50 ms test (237.8 ms before `unglue` was hand-scanned instead of a lookbehind). The renderer (`src/ask/AnswerText.tsx`) owns the elements and imports the parser's `Block` and `InlineToken`; nothing else in `src/` draws answer markdown, and the corpus (15 real answers under `src/agent/__tests__/fixtures/answers/`) is where a new shape a bench turns up gets pinned.

Holding a trailing pipe line back would leave a lone pipe line at the end of a final answer unrendered, which the grammar's own "a lone pipe line is prose" forbids. Probed on the wired `insight-stream` page with 30 deltas of the insight text cut at arbitrary characters: 0 settled violations; the tail flickered twice on an INCOMPLETE line (a lone `*` as prose for one delta before its `**…:**` closed, a lone backtick before its fence opened), which the invariant permits; a hold on a trailing marker-only line would remove both and is the parser's one-liner if the eye wants it.

- 2026-09-06 · **A rule that forbids a column has to say where the figure comes from instead** (HYBRID_RULES line 2, beside the untouched `- Return exactly the columns the question asks for, no extras.`): v3 already carried that column rule and Sonnet broke it three times in one shape, always to feed its own prose — `SELECT rating, COUNT(*)` for "return only the rating" (t3-08), `actor_id` and `film_count` beside the asked-for names (t4-04), `length` beside `rating, title` (t5-01), `total_amount` beside `currency, order_id` once on staging (s5-02) — because W5's "each ONE finding carrying its own figure" was the only line in the prompt that said where a figure may come from, and a column is the cheapest place to get one. So the new line names the three traps as traps (the column it orders by, the count it ranked with, an id) and then names the escape: a query already run or one more `run_sql`, never a column added to the final SQL. - 2026-09-06 · **The join fan-out is arithmetic, so the rule states the arithmetic before the remedy** (HYBRID_RULES line 3): `ph-05` joined rental and payment to customer in one pass, got `COUNT(DISTINCT r.rental_id)` right and `SUM(p.amount)` multiplied by the rental count, and failed identically in both v3 samples where v1 pre-aggregated each side in its own CTE and passed. The line says what happens (two one-to-many relations on the same parent multiply each other's rows and inflate every SUM and COUNT) before it says what to do (aggregate each in its own CTE, join the aggregates), because a model given only the instruction re-derives the one-pass join whenever the query gets long. Measured: two draws, both three CTEs and both PASS — and the hard bench's output tokens fell from 5375 / 6934 to 2024 / 3169 and 2346 / 2395, so v3's least careful SQL was also its most expensive.

Two facts in one line because they are the same trade the other two rules make: give the question what it asked for, in the type the arithmetic needs, instead of what looks complete. `ph-03` passes in both v4 draws with the numeric cast inside the LAG division; the row half is measured only in intent until a staging run exists.

The row itself is one row: `Edits in <connection>`, the switch at the right, the consequence sentence stacked under the label in the same row (the slider row's hint precedent) — "Ask can propose changes to this database; each runs only when you press its button, in a query tab you commit or roll back." NULL wears the grid's own chip on the old side. INSERT shows the after rows plain and DELETE the before rows plain — every cell of an inserted row is new, and a wash over all of them is noise — so the one red thing in the block stays the button. The grid grew exactly ONE prop for this, `changed: ReadonlyMap<"row:col", string | null>`; a second grid, a second cell component, or a value pre-joined into one string were all rejected (the first breaks rule 14, the second re-authors the grid's hottest memoised component, the third makes the two tiers untypable). `estimateWidths` reads the OLD value's length too, so a preview column never clips its own arrow, and a preview skips the stored-width cache entirely, which holds one value per column where a preview cell holds two.

- 2026-09-06 · **The write gate is a SECOND ENTRY POINT, not a second gate**: `classify_write` (`agent.rs`) shares the read gate's parse, its prose/parse-error split, its statement count and its whole data sweep (`sweep_from`), and differs in exactly three places — the top statement must be one of INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE (MERGE refused: one statement, three verbs, and the preview draws one shape), the sweep starts one node BELOW the top so the allowed write is not read as its own nested write (`sweep_write`, a data-modifying CTE or a write in a sublink still refused beneath it), and a `FOR UPDATE` inside it is allowed (the statement takes row locks by definition; in read mode it stays refused, §8.1 unchanged). `run_sql` and `probe` still call `classify` and only `classify`: no tool can reach the write gate, ever, on any connection.

An INSERT gets `None` (every row it touches is new); a derivation failure fails the preview outright rather than quietly drawing an UPDATE as if it were an INSERT (LESSONS 9).

Without it the rename path — `upsert({ id, name, sql, profile_id })`, which is what SavedQueries and tabs.ts already send — would have silently cleared the `question` a quick-ask kept and the check on the row, and a COALESCE in SQL would have made `Remove Check` impossible instead. The names are already in the body, so naming them again in the step would be one fact in two slots. The step's `ms` is its own time, not the run's so far, and the step is absent entirely when the profile added nothing.
- 2026-09-06 · **What a code entry point attaches to a question rides the tags' own header and lives on the exchange**: `AskRequest.context` is one more line under `TAGGED BY THE USER:` (`tab "cohort retention":` then the statement, `check "…": 12 rows · expected 0`) rather than a second block, because it is the same fact, what this question came with (DESIGN rule 15). It is stored as `Exchange.context` and read back by `runInto`, so Retry, Fix It, Restart and a chip toggle all ask with it again: a re-run that dropped it would ask about a query nobody sent. The drift line is `driftLabel(q)`, the same string the failed palette row prints, read once (LESSONS 13).
- 2026-09-06 · **`Explain with Ask` and `Ask Why` are two thin faces of `ask()`, not two paths**: `explainWithAsk(sql, tabName)` asks `Explain this query @"<tab>"` with the statement (capped at the tags' own 1500 characters, saying so when it cuts) under the tags; `askWhy(check)` asks `Why did @"<name>" fail its check?` and lets the ordinary saved-query tag carry the SQL, adding only the drift. A tab's own token is claimed by the tab, so a saved query of the same name cannot answer for it (the quoted ladder collides).
- 2026-09-06 · **The hint line's field IS the line, not a box beside it**: the rest face is a focusable div and the edit face a `textarea` (the table's line) or an `input` (a column's cell) wearing the same `.st-hint` padding, font and box, so opening one swaps only the ring and the fill on `--dur-quick` and no glyph moves; focus is the state's with `preventScroll` and the caret parks after the last word (LESSONS 7), never `autoFocus`, and blur saves as every inline editor in the app does.
- 2026-09-06 · **Define mode registers its own overlay layer instead of reading Escape**: escStack owns the key from a capture-phase window listener, so a mode living inside the palette earns its rung of the Esc ladder by pushing a layer (`EscLayer`), and Esc returns the palette with the query it held while a second Esc closes it.
- 2026-09-06 · **⌘↩ is read at the palette's cmdk root, which offers the key to its own handler first and stands down on a `preventDefault`**: the hot row is read where cmdk keeps it (`[cmdk-item][aria-selected="true"]`'s `data-value`), a quick-ask is asked again and a failed check asks why, and every other row leaves the key to cmdk, so the chord is ↩ everywhere else and never dead (LESSONS 9).
- 2026-09-06 · **A saved row's last three elements share one auto push**: `Ask Why` takes `margin-left: auto` and the drift and the 8px dot follow 4px apart, so a passing row's lone dot still parks at the right edge and a failed row's three never split the free space between them (the `.pal-detail ~ .pal-check` precedent), and `.linkish`'s own side margins are zeroed so the row keeps its 4px rhythm.
- 2026-09-06 · **`Run Checks` reports only the checks that have actually run**: a check added since the last run would inflate a number nobody watched happen, so the detail counts the rows carrying a verdict and the toast prints the tally the run itself returned (LESSONS 13); an all-pass run reads `3 checks passed`, because `0 failed` is the norm speaking (DESIGN rule 11).
- 2026-09-06 · **appdb v8 is written `8 => knowledge_v8(&tx)?`, and no test names the literal version, so the merge was free to renumber it — it did not have to, A4 having added no migration** — `docs/AGENT-SPEC.md` §9, `AGENT-UX.md` §14/§15, `EVAL.md` §3/§4 and `CLAUDE.md`'s A2 line are the doc law this wave lands, verified against the shipped code by the final gate (one drift found and fixed: AGENT-UX §14 said the kind column touched its label at 64px, the pre-widening column was 56px, matching `ask.css`'s own comment and the `56 → 76` change itself).
- 2026-09-06 · **The `knowledge` step's label is the one trace label that WRAPS, and each count is held whole across the break**: every other label is data read off the run (a statement's first line, a tool call) where a cut is honest overflow, but this one is a summary the chrome wrote, and `2 hints · 1 definition · 1 syn…` is not a summary of anything (the context step's tagged line, same drawer, wraps for exactly this reason). Only the knowledge row takes `white-space: normal` and a `min-height` in place of its fixed 28px, so every other row is byte-identical and the 560 frame does not move; a non-breaking space inside each count means the line can break only at a `·`, which costs the 320 floor a third line and buys a reader who never meets `1` stranded from `synonym`.
- 2026-09-06 · **`.pal-askwhy`'s auto push is written at the row's own specificity, `.pal [cmdk-item] .pal-askwhy`**: `.linkish` (tokens.css) sets `margin` at one class and `main.tsx` imports `App` before `tokens.css`, so a bare `.pal-askwhy` lost the tie and left the failed row's tail packed against the name while every passing row's dot sat at the right edge. A shared-property collision between a species class and a surface class is decided by import order, which no CSS file can see: the surface writes at its own nesting depth, as every other rule in that block already does.
- 2026-09-06 · **Every knowledge and check write cues both ends, and `copyCueError` is the one idiom for the failing one**: `Expect Current Shape` runs the query once and says what it learned (`Expecting 0 rows`, `Expecting 4`, `Expecting some rows`, from `expectCue`, `driftLabel`'s vocabulary said forward), `Remove Check` says `Check removed`, and a refused write, a dropped column or a timed-out run prints the error's first line instead of the surface snapping silently back (LESSONS 9). The cue is at the call site, never in the store, because that is where every other cue in this app lives; `copyCueError` in `src/lib/copyCue.ts` avoids what would have been a fifth private copy of `firstLine` (four already stand in `loop.ts`, `tools.tauri.ts`, `agent.ts` and `checks.ts`; none of the four is folded onto it this wave, LESSONS 13).

### Agent A3 · canvas (2026-09-06; picture: the "A3 · canvas" rows of ~/projects/qwry-agent-lab/docs/ask-sketch-a3.html)

- 2026-09-06 · **A canvas result block folds the assumption chips into its status line as fragments after one lowercase lead (`4 rows · 311.8 ms · assumed Last Month = August 2026 · Revenue = Paid Orders`, the labels tier 1, the lead and numbers tier 2), 5 non-row lines → 4**: a chip on the canvas toggles nothing (no retry pill lives there), and a `.chip.active` that answers no click is a control costume on a non-control (DESIGN rule 8's inverse); the trace's `tagged order_v2 · "Monthly revenue"` line (AGENT-UX §5) is the same fact's record form and the precedent for a toggle in the live pane becoming a status fragment in the record. Status folded into the question line's right end was weighed and refused: two registers on one line, and the line is the block's title (rule 12).
- 2026-09-06 · **An exchange's prose rides INSIDE the canvas result block, read-only, above the faces (question line · prose · face · status): one exchange is one block, never two**: dropping the sentence on Add to Canvas loses the reading the block was added for, and a note-plus-result pair per exchange carries the question line twice (rule 14) and two clusters. The model's words are provenance (LESSONS 4); the user's annotation is a note beneath, or an Ask.
- 2026-09-06 · **Canvas blocks wear no frame of their own (3 hairline boxes → 0 at rest); the `.rb` keeps its edge because a grid needs one**: Freeform frames nothing, a box per block is one chrome frame × n, and the hover cluster is the block's only chrome. Page padding 20, blocks 16 apart, parts 8 apart; a note's measure caps at 680 while its box spans the column (growth feeds content, and a 1250px line is worse content); the question line is a 24px row so the cluster centres on it, and a note with no question line takes the cluster 2px up on its first line (the answer cluster's own number).
- 2026-09-06 · **The chart is reached by the ONE flip glyph in a cycle, table → chart → SQL, the glyph the face you will get (`BarChart3` on the table, `Code` on the chart, `Table` on the SQL), never by a chart button of its own**: a second toggle over three faces has no rule for where Flip goes from the chart; the cycle keeps W7's glyph rule and adds 0 buttons, at the cost of one extra press from table to SQL. The chart stands second because a chartable result is added for its chart. On the canvas the chosen face is a fact of the document and persists in `doc_json`; in Ask it stays a way of looking (W7, never persisted).
- 2026-09-06 · **Bars lie down: a label axis draws horizontal bars, labels right-aligned to meet them in the status register, the value at every bar's end in the data register, no value axis**: nine thirteen-character labels under standing bars collide at 640 and would rotate or drop (rule 13, nothing clips at the floor); as rows they read like the grid's, and the value at the end retires the axis (rule 14). One scale across series (two scales in one plot lie); the accent ladder is three steps of one hue (100 · 45 · 22% over the panel); a legend appears from two series as one status-register line inside the face, top-left, none for one; the face is absent, never a message, over 200 rows, over three numeric columns, or with no label column. A date label draws a line instead, three y ticks and sparse x ticks, both status register.
- 2026-09-06 · **The diff face takes the table face's place while a comparison stands (the table's cells are the diff's A cells, rule 14), so the cycle stays three: diff → chart → SQL**: each numeric cell `731 · 748 · +2.3%` with the separators in tier 3, Δ the signed RELATIVE change at one decimal (an absolute difference restates two numbers already in the cell), A and B printed exactly as the database gave them; a row present on one side only wears the warn glyph on its label and `∅` for the side it lacks, no Δ; the status line `6 rows · staging 412.6 ms · prod 388.1 ms · assumed …` names both sides once (LESSONS 4), and over the cap on either side the face is that one line and nothing else.
- 2026-09-06 · **The result block's cluster is Copy · Flip · Insert · Ask · More (5 hot, 0 at rest); the note's is Copy · Ask · More (3 hot, 0 at rest); More is the block's menu, Compare With ▸ · Move Up · Move Down · Delete, the same menu a right-click opens, with `⌫` on a focused block as Delete (the Threads precedent)**: place actions (move, delete) act on the block's position in the document, not on its content, so they leave the cluster for the menu; Move Up is disabled, never hidden, on the first block (rule 2). Compare With ▸ stands on the canvas alone: a fourth block action in Ask is 4 × 18 + 3 × 4 + 16 + 4 = 104px over a 296px grid header at 320, a column name under the fade at the floor, and the pane is the conversation while the canvas is where things stand side by side.
- 2026-09-06 · **The empty canvas is empty: 0 strings, 0 controls, a text cursor over the card, a click writes the first note, and the palette's `New Note` is the keyboard route (rule 8)**: `Add an answer from Ask, or write a note.` was 1 string against 0 and dead on deletion (rule 11), Add to Canvas announces itself in every answer's cluster, and the Ask empty state already stands with no sentence (§1). The space under the last block is a tail of at least 64px that takes the same click. With three blocks the canvas carries 0 always-visible controls and 0 strips of its own; the tab is the strip's.
- 2026-09-06 · **Add to Canvas joins the answer's cluster as a third action (`LayoutGrid`, the tab's own glyph): 2 → 3 hot, 0 at rest**: Save Query and Add to Canvas do not merge (a saved query is the editor's artefact, a block the document's, and a `Save ▾` is a menu for two rows); 82px over the first line at 320, the prose dissolving under the fade as it does today. Cues `Added to Canvas` / `Added to a new canvas`; the pane does not switch tabs.
- 2026-09-06 · **Canvas motion adds no preset: a block arrives on `panelIn` (the card's own entrance) with the blocks under it making room on `spring.layout`; a face flips as W7's block flips (`swapIn` crossfade, real height on `spring.layout`, everything below riding it); a removed block fades where it stands while the gap closes on `spring.layout` (the fold's two registers); a note entering edit moves nothing, the box's border fading in on `--dur-quick`; the cluster reveals as every cluster does (opacity + 4px slide, `--dur-quick` / `--ease-std`)**; reduced motion is the block at once, the other face at once, the removed block gone at once.

- 2026-09-06 · **A canvas block is the `@` ladder's FIFTH RUNG, not a new argument to `ask()`**: `ask(question)` takes a string in this tree and the seam that already carries "here is a thing I mean" is `mentionsFor` / `resolveMentions`, so `agent/mentions.ts` gains a `block` kind with a `BlockRef` (id, name, sql, columns, rowCount, a note's text) and `MentionCtx` an optional `blocks` list the caller hands over like every other kind. It resolves LAST, under table, column, saved query and thread, because it is the only rung naming something the user built rather than something the connection has: a saved query and a block of one name still send the saved query, exactly as before the canvas existed. The token is the block's own name quoted (`@"can you check the revenue in last month"`, `canonicalToken("block", …)`, quotes doubled and read back by `parseMentions`), so the pill in the bubble is the existing `.mention` face for free, the trace's `tagged` line lists it beside the rest, and the popover's kind-icon map gains `LayoutGrid` for the day a row is offered. `mentionContext` sends the block's question, its statement in a fence and the SHAPE of its rows, never the rows: the block stands on the canvas beside the answer (DESIGN rule 14).
- 2026-09-06 · **The blocks a question may name live in `stores/ask.ts` beside the pane's other chrome, and `useAgent.askAbout(block)` is the one door**: the resolver is runtime-free by law (AGENT-SPEC §2 rule 2) so it can never hold them itself, and `stores/ask.ts` already owns the drafts a token is written into. `askAbout` registers the block, opens the pane in Ask through `useSidePane.show` (a request to write, never a radio press), and appends `@"name" ` to that CONNECTION's draft through a new `prefill(profileId, text)`, keyed by profile rather than by whatever composer is mounted, because `Ask` on a block fires before AskPanel is necessarily mounted and a draft that waited for a mount is a draft the user watches vanish. A space always joins the token to what came before it: the grammar reads `a@b` as one word and never a tag. `forgetBlock` is the canvas's way to drop a deleted block, whereupon its name is plain text again and the question still runs (LESSONS 5).
- 2026-09-06 · **`Exchange.askedFrom` is the exchange's own provenance, and it is where the pairing HAS to live**: `Ask` on a block runs before any exchange exists, so a map keyed by exchange id cannot be filled at that moment; the tag in the question is the only record there is, and it is also the record when the token is typed by hand rather than pressed. `ask()` reads the first `block` mention it resolved and stamps the id on the exchange, so Add to Canvas on the reply lands the new block under the one it answered (A3 item 4). Session-lived, never persisted: a reloaded thread has no answer to "where did this come from" and says so by having none.
- 2026-09-06 · **Ask reaches the canvas through one registered port (`src/canvas/port.ts`), never an import**: the pane is the conversation and the canvas is the document, and a hard import each way is how two surfaces become one knot; the port also keeps the canvas store out of the pane's bundle until something asks for it (Palette's `import("../stores/heal")` precedent). `stores/canvas.ts` calls `setCanvasPort` at its module scope; `loadCanvasPort()` loads it on the first walk through. The contract is one line wide: `addExchange(exchangeId)` and nothing else, because everything a block shows is already on that exchange in `useAgent` and a second copy passed through the door would be the same facts in two shapes (DESIGN rule 14). It answers with exactly what the cue must say, `ok` · `created` · `message`, so `Added to Canvas` / `Added to a new canvas` is never a cheerful lie over an add that landed nothing (LESSONS 9).
- 2026-09-06 · **A note is read and edited in ONE geometry, so entering edit moves no glyph AT ITS FIRST LINE**: the read view already carries the composer box's padding under a transparent border and pays both back in margin (`-9px -13px`, the `.mention` pill's own outdent one step larger), so the box grows OUTWARD when it appears and the first glyph's origin holds; edit fades a border in around the words on `--dur-quick`. Below that first line the claim narrows: a rendered lead or list has its own line boxes, and the textarea's source lines are not the same boxes, so a multi-line note's later lines shift by their own spacing (2 to 6 CSS px, measured) even though nothing scrolls or jumps at the read/edit boundary itself. The sketch drew the box's padding as an inset, which would have shifted every glyph 12px right on a click, against its own motion note. The note's cluster is `Copy` · `Ask` · `More` (3 hot, 0 at rest); `Delete…` earns its ellipsis through the app's danger confirm (`Delete Note?` · the note's first line in typographic quotes · `Delete Note`), while a note emptied in edit deletes itself on commit with no dialog, the fold's own contract that the preview IS the commit.
- 2026-09-06 · **`Compare With ▸` reuses the app's context-menu primitive and takes its list from the store that runs the comparison**: the picker is `compareWithMenu`, one `MenuNode` built over `compareTargets(profileId)`, so the rows the user picks from and the connections the diff can actually run on cannot drift apart. The check on the compared sibling rides the row's HINT slot, which is where the app's menu rows already carry a mark; a check column of its own would be a fourth slot on every menu row in the window for one submenu's sake. With no sibling the row stands disabled, never hidden (DESIGN rule 2's matrix), so the action stays discoverable on a one-connection machine.
- 2026-09-06 · **The canvas is framed through the harness's SECOND ROOT, `?harness=canvas`, at the MAIN card's widths (640 · 960 · 1280) in a 760-tall card, and `ask-frames.ts` grows one `--harness` flag rather than a second script**: the pane's 320 · 392 · 560 in a 640-tall card are the pane's, and a canvas shot at them would be evidence for a surface that does not exist; a second script would fork the CDP driver, the ready mark, the settle and the reduced-motion flags into two copies of one thing (DESIGN rule 14). `mountCanvasHarness` seeds `useCanvas` from `fixtures.canvas.ts` with `loaded` already true (so `CanvasTab`'s own mount effect never asks the shim for a list), seeds two connections so `Compare With ▸` has a sibling to offer, and stamps the hot block one frame after mount, the `result` states' precedent. The two states the canvas changes ABOUT the pane (`a3-add`, `a3-ask-block`) stay on the Ask root, since their subject is the pane's own cluster and bubble. `tauriShim` answers `canvas_list` · `canvas_upsert` · `canvas_delete` and the comparison's `agent_connect` · `agent_run_readonly` · `disconnect` with canned rows, and records every upsert, so a probe reads back what a click actually wrote rather than what the DOM implies.
- 2026-09-06 · **Which connection a canvas tab belongs to is the DOCUMENT's fact, read from the canvas list, never from the tab row's own `profile_id`**: `openCanvasTab` stamps whatever connection was active when the tab opened, while Add to Canvas lands on the EXCHANGE's connection, and `currentFor` compared the two — so whenever they differed (a thread of one connection while another is active, and every harness page, which has no active connection at all) every add opened a SECOND canvas and cued `Added to a new canvas` forever. `currentFor` now takes the connection's own canvas ids and keeps the tabs that show one, which is the same fact in one slot (DESIGN rule 14, LESSONS 4). Caught by the integration probe, not by a test: both store tests happened to run with the active connection equal to the exchange's.
- 2026-09-06 · **A reply's home on the canvas is resolved from `Exchange.askedFrom` alone**: `useCanvas.addExchange` falls back from its session map to the block id the exchange itself carries, scanning the documents for the canvas that holds it, so the pairing works for a hand-typed `@"name"` as well as for a press of `Ask`, and `noteAskedFrom` is left as the door for a caller that has both ids. The other half is the release: `remove` and `deleteCanvas` call `useAsk.forgetBlock`, so a deleted block stops resolving and its name in an older bubble goes back to plain text with nothing refusing (LESSONS 5).
- 2026-09-06 · **A removed canvas block fades on `panelIn`'s own spring, not on `--dur-slow`, and AGENT-UX §16g is amended to say so**: the locked motion decision asked for the fold's two registers, a `--dur-slow` fade over a `spring.layout` gap, but there is no way to write 240ms into a `motion` exit that this tree allows — a raw duration in a component is against the motion law (springs.ts presets and `--dur` tokens only) and a `blockOut` preset is against the same decision's own headline, that canvas motion adds NO preset. An opacity-only leave is indistinguishable between a 240ms ease and PANEL, so the rule that still meant something won and the block leaves the way it arrived (DESIGN rule 15's rules-are-floors clause: cite the rule, say why, amend it in the same pass).
- 2026-09-06 · **A canvas tab's kind on disk is one nullable `tabs.canvas_id`, never a `kind` column**: the tabs table holds query tabs today and a kind column would have to be backfilled onto every existing row with a value none of them chose, where non-NULL-is-the-kind adds one nullable column and reads back exactly (`r.canvas_id ? "canvas" : "query"`); table tabs stay session-only and need no column at all, so one migration arm (written `8 => canvases_v8`, landed by the merge as `9 => canvases_v9`) carries the whole of item 1's persistence.
- 2026-09-06 · **`doc_json` is opaque to appdb and the whole document is one debounced write (400ms), never a row per block**: the block shape is the canvas store's and a schema that followed it would migrate on every field added to a block, so appdb stores the JSON and hands it back verbatim; one write also means a reorder, a persisted face flip and an edited note move together or not at all, where per-block rows would let a crash land half a reorder.

- 2026-09-06 · **Whether a chart face exists is a store rule (`chartOf`), and `facesOf` is the flip cycle, so Flip can never land on a face that is not there**: the availability test (one label column, one to three numeric columns, one to 200 rows, a date label drawing a line) is the same fact the renderer needs and the cycle needs, and holding it once means the UI cannot draw a chart the cycle refuses to reach or skip one it offers.

- 2026-09-06 · **A right-click inside the result block's faces box belongs to the FACE, not to the block: the grid retargets its selection and opens the cell menu there, and the block's own menu answers everywhere else on the block**: the grid's handler does not stop propagation, so a block-level handler would open two menus on one press; `More` is the block menu's always-there route (DESIGN rule 8), so nothing is lost.

- 2026-09-08 · **COMMIT and ROLLBACK get ONE implementation, `endTabTx(key, "commit" | "rollback")` in `stores/connections.ts`**, run straight on the tab's session (never through `run()`, which would wipe the grid mid-transaction) — the status bar's `TX OPEN` chip's own rollback button was rewritten onto this call rather than kept as a second copy (rule 15's consolidate-before-you-add; a second copy is the founding sin rule 1 exists to prevent). It stamps a session-lived `txEnds` map (tab key → the end sent) before the round trip, cleared the moment a fresh transaction opens on that tab, and a `useConnections` subscriber in `agent.ts` (`stampTxEnd`) writes `exchange.ranTx` onto every `ran` exchange pointing at that tab the instant `txTabs[key]` flips false. So the block learns from the TAB, not from its own button, and a transaction closed some other way (a typed `COMMIT`, a dead session) stamps nothing — the headline falls back to the reload-shaped line rather than guess (LESSONS 9). `useAgent.commitWrite` / `rollbackWrite` are the block's own two callers, refused unless the exchange's own tab still holds the transaction.

- 2026-09-08 · **Gates, re-run clean after the fix above**: `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean · `bun scripts/design-lint.ts` 0 finding(s) · `bun test` 820 pass / 0 fail (52 files, 7538 expect() calls). Every AGENT-UX §1/§1a, DESIGN rule 12 and taste-gate SKILL.md amendment the wave's findings named was read back against the shipped code (`mentionRows.ts`, `fuzzy.ts`, `MentionPopover.tsx`, `Mention.tsx`, `AskPanel.tsx`, `ask.css`, `stores/recents.ts`, `stores/tabs.ts`) and matches; no further drift found. All five `b2-` fixtures re-shot at 320 · 392 · 560 in both themes into the scratchpad's `b2-frames/` (30 files), plus six regression states (`mention-first` · `mention-draft` · `mention-echo` · `disconnected` · `small` · `busy`, 36 files): none moved from their pre-B2 shape by eye.

### Agent B3 · the canvas the model writes into (2026-09-09; picture: the "B3 · canvas" section of ask-sketch-b3.html)

- 2026-09-09 · **The MCP canvas tools are a bridge, not an implementation**: `agent_mcp.rs` already mirrors the five tools' result text line for line in Rust, ~470 lines that must change twice or drift; mirroring three more (block ids, chart availability, the row caps, the document's own rules) would double that debt for the one path that cannot call TypeScript directly. So Rust holds ZERO canvas semantics: `canvas_call` mints a `call_id`, parks a `oneshot`, emits `canvas-tool-call` and waits 20 s, and `src/agent/canvas.tauri.ts` answers with the very text the HTTP path returns. Cost: one IPC round trip per canvas call, against one implementation, one id-minting site and one place a cap can be wrong.
- 2026-09-09 · **The served tool list is the gate, so a no-target token cannot reach a canvas tool**: `agent_mcp_serve(session_id, timeout_ms, tools)` binds the list to the TOKEN, which is minted per `chat()` and revoked in the adapter's own `finally`, and the adapter hands it `req.tools.map(t => t.name)` — the same array `toolsFor(!!req.canvas)` built for every other provider. One condition, both paths: a run with no target serves the byte-identical five and the child never learns the family exists. A tool that is always visible and sometimes refuses was refused instead: W7's prose spiral is what a refusal the model can see costs.
- 2026-09-09 · **The bridge answers every call it can see, and says which kind of nothing it found**: a `canvas-tool-call` for a session with a live registration is answered by that session's tools; one for a session whose tools have been disposed is answered `ERROR: the canvas is not open for this thread`. Dropping it would spend the child's whole 20 s and then tell it `the canvas did not answer`, which is a worse account of the same fact (LESSONS 9). One registration per session id, replaced by a newer exchange and removed only by its own stop, and one Tauri listener for the app while any session is registered.
- 2026-09-09 · **The caret on an empty canvas is the PAGE's, not the document's**: activating an empty canvas tab puts a caret at the first line's origin, and that line is a local draft, so nothing empty ever reaches `doc_json` and a stray click cannot mint a block. The three stacked boxes the maintainer saw were `CanvasTab`'s own `write()` minting an empty note per click and persisting each inside the 400 ms debounce; a click now places ONE caret line, and clicking five times places the same one.
- 2026-09-09 · **Blur commits a note with words and removes an empty one**: the fold's own contract, applied one step earlier (an empty commit already deleted, `NoteBlock`'s own rule). A tab switch or a click on the page therefore never loses typed words and never leaves a box behind. The other reading, blur cancels, loses the words a person just typed, which is the one thing a document may not do.
- 2026-09-09 · **A note in edit gains the ring alone: no fill, no border step**: `1px solid var(--accent)` from the first frame, fading in on `--dur-quick`, over the page's own background rather than `.ask-box`'s `--bg-app` card; an empty note in edit has no ring at all, since a ring around nothing is the rounded box with a thinner line. The words do not move onto a darker surface to be edited, which is what made the canvas read as a form.
- 2026-09-09 · **The `@` ladder's seventh kind, `canvas`, sends no context of its own**: `@"Canvas 4"` resolves to the exchange's target and contributes NO line to `TAGGED BY THE USER:`, because the `CANVAS:` block is where the canvas is described, once (rule 14), and it describes it better (the title AND the outline the model may edit against). Promoted out of B2's `BlockRef.canvas` flag: a whole canvas is a different noun from a block, and only one of the two can be a target.
- 2026-09-09 · **One row cap for a canvas document, 200, on both routes**: `Add to Canvas` kept up to `UI_ROW_CAP` (2,000) while a model write kept 200, two numbers for one idea in one `doc_json` blob. The press now truncates at the same 200 and says so in the block's own status line, `200 of 1,842 rows`, which replaces A3's `· showing 2,000` fragment: one sentence, one place (`capRun`).
- 2026-09-09 · **The prefilled pill announces, and never takes the caret**: an empty draft beside an active canvas tab is written straight into `useAsk.drafts` rather than through `prefill`, whose `focusSeq` bump would pull focus into the composer the same frame the empty canvas is giving the PAGE its caret. A pill is a statement about where the answer will land, not a request to start typing.
- 2026-09-09 · **`restartConfirmText` lives in the store, beside the count it reads**: the older-Restart confirm now counts canvas blocks as well as questions and answers (`The 2 questions after this one, their answers and 7 canvas blocks will be deleted.`), and the number comes from the exchanges' own `canvasWrites`. The string moved out of `EchoActions.tsx` so the sentence and its arithmetic cannot drift apart (LESSONS 13).
- 2026-09-09 · **A model's title and a user's question keep their own fields and share one slot**: `ResultBlock.title` is the model's ≤6-word line, `question` the exchange's own; `titleOf` renders whichever the block has, so the block's first line is one slot (rule 14) while the document still records who wrote the words (LESSONS 4). Storing the model's title in the field named `question` would make a later reader, and `outline()`, report it as something the user asked.
- 2026-09-09 · **`facesOf` offers `values` only up to four columns**: `ScalarResult` draws at most `SCALAR_MAX_COLS` pairs, so a one-row result of five or more columns is a grid; offering a face the run cannot stand on would be a flip to nothing (DESIGN rule 11). One number, imported from the component that draws it rather than typed twice.
- 2026-09-09 · **The canvas pill is the ONE target; an active canvas tab aims nothing** (`aimCanvas`, stores/agent.ts): the ambient route that aimed at the active canvas tab when a question carried no pill is gone, so `aimCanvas` is the pill, then the word `canvas` on a connection with none, then no target. The tab's whole job is `syncCanvasPill`'s prefill, which the ⌫ the code already promised can now actually decline (AGENT-UX §16l), and a bubble with no pill in it is an answer that stayed in the pane. A destination the bubble does not name is a destination the reader cannot audit (LESSONS 4) or refuse (LESSONS 7). Consequence left open for the maintainer: a FOLLOW-UP pressed under a canvas answer sends its own words with no pill (`onPick` → `ask(q)`), so it is answered in the pane; carrying the last exchange's pill into that press would keep it on the canvas and is one line, but it is a route AGENT-UX §16l does not list yet.
- 2026-09-09 · **A busy thread refuses a question BEFORE the aim is read**: `ask()` captures the thread and returns on `busy` above `aimCanvas`, because route 2 creates a canvas, opens its tab and shows a cue — side effects a question that asks nothing must not have (LESSONS 3). The capture also feeds both `mentionsFor` calls, so the tags and the run read one moment.
- 2026-09-09 · **`canvasTools` asks the document for a block's faces, never a second copy of the rule** (`facesFor` → `facesOf(candidate())`, canvas.tauri.ts): the tool used to decide `values` for any one-row run and `table` for any run with rows, so a 1×5 result was told `values face` while `blockFromModel` landed it on the table. The document answers, and a fixed `FACE_ORDER` (chart · values · table · sql) turns that answer into the tool's preference, so a face the reply names is the face the block stands on (LESSONS 13).

### Agent A3 · B3 (the canvas as the agent's surface, 2026-09-09; picture: the B3 rows of ask-sketch-b3.html; research: qwry-agent-lab/docs/research/canvas-agent.md and canvas-agent-spec.md)

- 2026-09-09 · **Five claims verified against the shipped tree, all RESOLVED, four of them with no code moved.** (1) `EchoActions.tsx` imports `restartConfirmText` from the store instead of defining its own, pinned by `agent-canvas.test.ts:592-596`. (2) `aimCanvas` (`stores/agent.ts:2380`) has exactly two routes — a canvas pill, or the literal word `canvas` on a connection with no canvas tab — and never reads the active tab as a target, so a hand-cleared prefilled pill genuinely declines the destination rather than silently keeping it. (3) `facesFor` (`canvas.tauri.ts:137`) now derives from `facesOf(candidate(...))`, the one rule the block itself uses, closing the gap where a one-row five-column result was told `values face` by the tool while the block landed on `table`; both now agree on `table`. (4) The busy guard in `ask()` runs before `aimCanvas`, so a question a busy thread refuses creates no canvas and cues nothing. (5) The one real drift: the composer's own `@"Canvas 4"` pill rendered as plain text rather than the icon pill in the `b3-ask-summary` fixture, because the harness seeded no active canvas tab for `canvasTabRefs` to resolve against; `fixtures.b3ask.ts` now seeds one, and the corrected stills replace the stale set below.
- 2026-09-09 · **Closing this against the ten maintainer calls**: calls 1–3 (two block kinds with the values face standing in for a metric row; exactly three tools, `canvas_write`/`canvas_replace`/`canvas_read`, no create and no delete; one targeting mechanism, the `@"Canvas N"` pill, with the app minting a canvas exactly once on the literal word) and calls 4–5 (the 200-row cap shared by both routes; assumptions patched only onto an exchange's first result block) stand as the "Agent B3" block above already records, unchanged by this pass. Call 6 (the pane's four-line canvas exchange, no answer text or cluster) is now pixel-verified: the re-shot `b3-ask-summary` stills show the bubble's pill, the strip's `canvas ×2` chip and `4 blocks · Canvas 4` with the title linkish, nothing else. Calls 7–8 (the note's ring-only edit state; `wroteBy` provenance on every model block) read correctly off `NoteBlock.tsx` and `stores/canvas.ts`. Calls 9–10 (the MCP bridge as the only implementation; `$ref`/`$defs` verified dead on all three wire formats, the union inlined twice rather than three times) are `agent_canvas.rs` and `tools.schema.json` as shipped, both pinned by the tests the builder's own block names.
- 2026-09-09 · **One doc drift found and closed, no code moved**: `ROADMAP.md`'s A3 tick (the 2026-09-06 canvas-of-blocks item) had not been told B3 extended it — no clause naming the three tools or the analysis mode, and its open-items list still read as if a canvas-targeted answer had a presentation score. Both fixed in place; B3's own tick stays filed under A1.5, where the wave actually ran, since a citation is not a move.
- 2026-09-09 · **Gates, re-run clean**: `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean · `bun scripts/design-lint.ts` 0 finding(s) · `bun test` 993 pass / 0 fail (60 files, 8,180 `expect()` calls) · `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean · `cargo test` 126 pass / 0 fail (35 ignored, live-DB/staging suites). `localhost:1420` (the maintainer's own dev build) answered 200 throughout this pass; no vite of this pass's own was left running on 1429.
- 2026-09-09 · **The `b3-ask-summary` frame seeds the canvas TAB its composer pill resolves against**: a draft's tags resolve against `canvasTabRefs`, so without an open `Canvas 4` tab the prefilled pill framed as plain text while the product would paint a pill. The bubble's pill needs no tab (it is minted from `canvasWrites`); the composer's does, and the frame is now the state the prefill actually comes from — an active canvas tab beside an empty draft (DESIGN rule 9).

### Perf · B5 (2026-09-09)

- 2026-09-09 · **@ popover row memo (mentionRows.ts, MentionPopover.tsx): keystrokes missing the 16 ms budget fell from 5 of 12 to 2 of 12.** sectionsFor now caches every MentionRow by source-object identity (a WeakMap per kind, column rows keyed under their own table, the five category rows as module constants), and Row is memo'd behind a stable onTake ref, so a row the new filter still holds bails out with its cmdk item and its glyph. Keystroke 3 21.2 → 13.6 ms, keystroke 4 17.7 → 9.9 ms, keystroke 5 17.3 → 9.2 ms; Row renders 356 → 131, cmdk forwardRefs 841 → 389, kind glyphs 333 → 123 over the same 12 keystrokes. Motion untouched (menuIn, the entry, the redraw-in-place); 24 popover harness frames byte-identical.
- 2026-09-09 · **Palette left the entry chunk as a tenth lazy(), and the five-chunk idle warm-up now mounts it closed instead of merely preloading it (App.tsx).** Eager payload 709.03 kB raw / 220.94 kB gzip → 646.56 kB / 200.50 kB (-8.8% / -9.3%), entry source modules 466 → 419. Cold start unmoved within noise (168 → 164 ms median, 0 long tasks either side): the cut is banked headroom, not a startup win. The idiomatic `{open && <Suspense>}` shape was tried and rejected first: it throttled ⌘K to 320-330 ms against 34.6-36.2 ms with the static import; mounting the palette closed from the idle pass keeps ⌘K a prop flip on an already-resolved component, 29.1-45.8 ms. Palette.tsx itself is byte-unchanged.
- 2026-09-09 · **ResultBlock formats SQL where it is read, not at mount, and the format memoizes per (sql, preset) (ResultBlock.tsx, format.ts).** A 40-exchange thread's mount fell 302.6 → 251.2 ms task (-17%), 128.1 → 99.6 ms script; React commits 4 → 3. sql-formatter is no longer fetched at all on a first result block (before: 439.7 kB starting 288 ms after nav). Flip median 41.5 ms → 25.7/33.3 ms. Copy and Insert still hand over formatted SQL from any face, and formatSqlText's unparseable-statement fallback survives, memoized with everything else; formatSqlText is now a private door behind formattedSql/formattedSqlPeek. Motion untouched (swapIn, spring.layout, popLayout, layout="position"); 22 harness frames bit-for-bit identical.
- 2026-09-09 · **The formatter chunk now carries one dialect instead of 24 (new file src/editor/formatterChunk.ts).** 293.41 kB raw / 76.33 kB gzip → 71.74 kB / 19.59 kB (-75.6% / -74.3%); 0 occurrences of bigquery, singlestoredb, clickhouse, transactsql, snowflake, duckdb or mariadb in the emitted chunk. A one-line re-export naming formatDialect and postgresql alone is what keeps the other 23 out, since sql-formatter's own sideEffects:false lets rollup drop the unreferenced grammars; no deep path into dist/esm, no version pin, no new dependency.
- 2026-09-09 · **The canvas memoizes its block list by block id (CanvasTab.tsx, new CanvasRow component): a face flip re-renders the block that flipped, not the document.** Steady-state flip main thread 59.4 → 49.3 ms pooled (-17%), 60.6 → 45.8 ms on a quiet interleaved run (-24%). Per flip: ResultBlock 9 → 2, AnswerText 13 → 2, MessageSquare/Ellipsis/Copy 12/12/13 → 0 (the whole document dropped to just the flipped block). Mount commits 5 → 4. Block order, caret position and the compare menu's rows unchanged; 66 canvas harness frames byte-identical. The `layout` + `arrive` spread + `exit` motion props moved into CanvasRow unchanged, not removed.
- 2026-09-09 · **Known item: display.test.ts's parse-budget test now reads min(20 rounds of 100 parses) instead of one 1000-parse wall clock; the test name and the 50 ms cap are unchanged.** The old reading hit 478 ms once under load (15.4x its own median), which would fail unchanged code; the new one held 28.3-29.6 ms under 8 spinners on 8 cores while the mean of the rounds rose to 97 ms and the worst round to 316. bun test names and counts stay identical (993 pass, 8180 expect() calls).
- 2026-09-09 · **Known item: tabs.ts gains cancelTabSaves() (src/stores/tabs.ts), the debounce/retry half canvas.ts's cancelCanvasSaves already had.** Reproduced with a standalone leak probe: before, tearing down window mid-debounce left one `tabs_save failed ReferenceError: window is not defined` with a 3 s retry armed against the torn-down transport; after, 0 console.error lines, retry armed false. Called in afterEach/afterAll of the four suites that mount tabs (canvas.test.ts, canvas-model.test.ts, agent-canvas.test.ts, agent-writes.test.ts); no product path calls it, since a window close flushes instead.
- 2026-09-09 · **Trial reverted: content-visibility: auto + contain-intrinsic-size on the 40-exchange mount's .ans rows.** Cut the mount 303.0 → 251.3 ms task (-17%), but paint containment clipped `.ans-echo::before`'s inset ring at the article's edge (echo-560-dark and actions-560-dark frames differ from control) and turned the scroll from 0 layout / 0 style recalc into 6.1 ms layout / 7.0 ms recalc over 18 layouts, the wrong direction on the one interaction that had been free. Reverted. An in-view gate on the result grid was measured as an alternative (same ~50% cut, no pixel cost) but not built: it needs scroll anchoring the shipping WKWebView cannot be leaned on for.
- 2026-09-09 · **Hotspots left for a later wave**: the 40-exchange mount still costs ~251 ms, of which ~120 ms is roughly 240 simultaneous `ans-in` entry fades (187.7 ms with them stripped under forced reduced motion vs 309.7 ms as shipped pre-fix) and is motion, out of scope this wave. The grid at 2000 rows re-renders ~143 of 320 windowed cells per scroll commit but holds 60 fps (0 frames over 20 ms), so it is a finding and not work. Lucide icon mass (57 modules / 13.9 kB in the entry, 88 distinct bundle-wide) re-renders with its parents; the re-render half is already cut wherever ranks 1 and 5's memoization reaches it, the bundle half is not where the 648.6 kB entry's weight lives.

### LAW · the canvas grid (C2, 2026-09-11; law in AGENT-UX.md sections 16o-16v, AGENT-SPEC.md 5.2/9, DESIGN.md rule 4; research: qwry-agent-lab/docs/research/canvas-grid.md and canvas-grid-spec.md; picture: the "C1 · the grid" section of ask-sketch-c1.html)

- 2026-09-11 · **The cell is 108, not the sketch's 112, because the grid's container is the SCROLLER's content box and not the card.** A card of 640 lays out 640 − 2 (the card's hairline both sides) − 2 × 16 (the page inset, `canvas.css`'s 20 → 16) = 606, so `floor((606 + 12) / 120) = 5` columns at the floor, 7 at 960 and 10 at 1280. The product research doc's 112 reads 5 at the floor only by forgetting the border. The cell stretches to fill and is capped at 1.25 × base; whatever is left over widens the GUTTERS evenly, so the grid closes flush on both edges at every width and there is never a dead right margin.
- 2026-09-11 · **The row unit is FIXED at 108 with the same 12 gutter, and the engineering spec's 56 is refused.** Heights are set by content that does not scale with the window (a table row, a bar's pitch, a line of prose), so a second vertical unit would be two rulers for one idea. One consequence is accepted and visible in every frame: a block's box is quantised to 108 + 12, so a four-row table in a two-cell box has about a cell of slack under its last row. That is the dashboard-grid look and it is honest; a cut row is not.
- 2026-09-11 · **Where the two research docs disagree, the PRODUCT doc wins.** It carried 1 corner resize handle against the engineering doc's 3, spans keyed to a 5-column floor against a 4-column one, and `Move Up`/`Move Down` retired from the two `More` menus (result 4 rows → 3, note 3 → 1) rather than kept as one-row `moveTo` calls that move nothing a reader can see on a two-dimensional page (DESIGN rule 15). The store's `move(canvasId, id, delta)` is untouched and still does a one-row `moveTo` if the rows are ever wanted back.
- 2026-09-11 · **Reflow is MONOTONE and runs no trailing compaction, which is stronger than the spec's own property.** The spec says re-place with a first fit "then compact", and hole-filling provably breaks the property the same spec makes a gate: with A(3×2), B(3×1), C(2×1) at 5 columns, C fills the hole at (3,0) and the reading order becomes A,C,B, after which `readingOrder(reflow(reflow(L,4),10)) != readingOrder(reflow(L,10))`. Each element is therefore placed at the first free rect at or AFTER its predecessor's (y,x), which preserves the reading order exactly and makes the spec's stated property a corollary. `place()` for a NEW element still hole-fills, per spec. Checked against the c1-grid layout at 5 columns: monotone and hole-filling produce the identical layout there, so no frame moved.
- 2026-09-11 · **A drop is PINNED through the compaction, and a hole ABOVE an element can therefore stand.** Product research 4.5 reads the other way, but pinning is what makes a downward drag mean anything: if the dragged element also floats, dragging A below B pushes B down and then floats A straight back above it, a no-op gesture. The alternative (react-grid-layout's direction-sensitive push-away) buys the no-hole-above rule at the cost of a second rule inside one gesture, which is what 4.5 refused swap for. Open: the keyboard path can walk an element down a row per press, and the one-line clamp the engine proposed (`bounds(items, columns).rows`) does NOT bound it, because each press pushes the neighbour down and grows the page by the row it measured against.
- 2026-09-11 · **The document is v2 (`cell` per block, `autoH`, an advisory `lastColumns`) and its migration runs in MEMORY only.** Spec 2.5's second consequence ("opening an old canvas writes it") is refused in favour of maintainer call 7: migration is idempotent and cheap, so re-running it on every load costs nothing and a read never looks like an edit. A v1 document parses, lays out (every block full width in its stored order, capped at six cells, its kind's own height) and is never reported broken or written over until the reader's first change. No appdb migration was added; `doc_json` already held opaque JSON.
- 2026-09-11 · **A narrower window derives a layout; it never writes one.** The stored layout is held aside in memory while a narrower page renders the reflowed one, and it returns whole when the window does. An EDIT at the narrow count commits the derived layout and releases the held one, which is what makes an edit an edit (canvas-grid 4.6). The held layout is session-lived, so a reload at a narrow width re-derives from the stored layout but an edit there loses the wide layout for good. `setColumns` and `reflowTo` are memory-only for the same reason, where spec 2.4 lists them as plain `setDoc` actions.
- 2026-09-11 · **`at` and `span` are clamped and SAID, never refused** (`asked for 6 wide, the canvas is 5 columns, placed 5 wide`). A structurally malformed co-ordinate (a string, `{x}` with no `y`) IS refused with a sentence naming the way out: "clamped, never refused" covers a number the grid cannot honour, not a value that is not a co-ordinate, where there is nothing to clamp and inventing one would place a block somewhere the model never asked for. Fractions and negatives clamp silently, because neither is a statement about the canvas's size. The no-target run's tools and message stay byte-identical and `PROMPT_VERSION` does not move (the B3 pins).
- 2026-09-11 · **A result's span is read from the face it STANDS on, not the face it stores** (`spanKeyOf` asks `facesOf`). A one-row result filed under `table` cannot wear that face at all and renders its figures, so reading the stored name gave it the table's 6×4 and opened it four rows tall around one line of pairs (100px of air under the b3 fixture's figures block, visible in the b3-canvas-analysis frames before the fix).
- 2026-09-11 · **A result's height counts the PROSE standing above its face.** `30 + 26 × rows + 56` left out the sentence the model writes, so every result that carried one opened a line short and the grid cut its last row (a3-canvas at 640 lost row 4 of 4). `ContentSize.lines` now carries the prose's wrapped lines for the table, diff and SQL faces at 20px a line, measured at the width the block stands at, the same measure a note's words take.
- 2026-09-11 · **Counts, before and after: 0 always-visible controls per element and per page, 1 resize handle, 1 transient string during a gesture (`5 × 2`), 0 new springs presets, 0 new dependencies.** The per-kind toolset is the same `.acts-float` cluster with a `kindTools(kind)` membership, and the grip is its first action.
- 2026-09-11 · **The two gesture frames are PRESSED, not drawn** (fix round). The poses wrote `transform` on `.cvg-drag` and a box on `.cvg-ghost` by hand; both are motion values, so the React commit that follows re-applied their zeros and at 640 - where the layout is DERIVED and the measure commits one - the drag and the resize frames held no placeholder, no displaced neighbour and a clipped `1` from a zero-sized ghost at the card's corner, while 960 and 1280 (no commit after the pose) looked right. The fixture now dispatches the product's own `pointerdown`/`pointermove` on the grip and on the corner handle and leaves the gesture open, so the lift, the lattice, the placeholder, the size label and every neighbour the engine displaced are the product's answer. The one thing a synthetic pointer cannot do is take capture, and `setPointerCapture` is stubbed for the length of the press. The finger now stands 44 × 36 off the snap (was 9 × 14, a 5px sliver with the label under the element), left of the placeholder on a drag and short of the span on a resize, which is what puts the placeholder's own corner and its one string in view.
- 2026-09-11 · **A narrowing is measured against the LAYOUT's own right edge, never `lastColumns`.** Every window that merely opens a document raises `lastColumns` to its own width, so a layout authored at 7, opened once at 10 and shown again at 7 was re-flowed at the count it was made under: the honoured `x` was lost and the first edit persisted the rearrangement (open the canvas with Ask closed, then open Ask). `reflowTo` now derives only when the page is narrower than `max(x + w)`, and `lastColumns` stays what it was written to be, advisory, for the model's outline. AGENT-UX 16q says so in a clause.
- 2026-09-11 · **A derived width commits nothing while the column count holds.** `reflowTo` ran on every ResizeObserver frame and always `setDoc`'d fresh block objects for everything it had displaced, so a window drag inside one column band was a React render per frame with `CanvasRow`'s memo broken on every one of them - the opposite of 16r's two-custom-property promise, and only at the narrow width. It now compares the derived cells against the page as it stands, keeps the standing block objects where the flow lands them, and returns when nothing moved: 8 React commits over a four-step drag at 640 became 4, the two charts' own redraws, the same count the stored width already paid.
- 2026-09-11 · **The values face stands on the page's cells.** The row's span is one cell a pair and two for a figure past eight glyphs (`valueCells`), and the face itself still laid its pairs out on the retired 96px minimum with a 24 gap, so a width computed one way was drawn another (four pairs packed into 440px of a 606px element, the cells 124 apart under them). `.blk .rb-vals` is now `repeat(var(--w), minmax(0, 1fr))` with `var(--gut)` as the gap and the wide pair spanning two, and the glyph count is read once, by the file that prints the figure (`figureText` moved to ScalarResult, where `groupDigits` already lived).
- 2026-09-11 · **Every door that takes a height clamps it, and a rect no grid can hold loses its cell.** `fit()` ran `y` through `ROW_MAX` and left `h` alone, so a corrupt `h` allocated one occupancy row per cell of it and pushed its neighbours that far down (`h: 4e6` measured at 38 ms and 157 MB; `h: 1e9` is the window, and `parseDoc` carried that number in). `place`, `move`, `reflow`, `fit` and `bounds` now agree on `SPAN_MAX.h`, `validCell` rejects a height past it, and `columnsOfDoc` is capped at `COLUMNS_MAX` so its column test is a test rather than one the widest block sets for itself.

### Agent A3 · C2b fix round (the drawing and image context, 2026-09-12; picture: the "C1 · the grid" section of ask-sketch-c1.html)

- 2026-09-12 · **Maintainer call 1's cap is REFUSED and not evicted, and the law is amended to the tree.** The plan said the oldest stroke goes to make room for the newest; the element ships the opposite, refusing a stroke past 64 KB and standing `Full at 64 KB` at the sheet's foot. Eviction reads better as a sentence and worse as a drawing: it destroys work nobody is looking at, across a document write that has already happened, with no undo behind it, while a refusal loses only the stroke whose maker is watching the cue. AGENT-UX §16w now says so, the plan's wording recorded as the deviation it is; the tool half of its sentence waits on a tool that writes strokes, which this wave has none of (§16aa, open).
- 2026-09-12 · **Maintainer call 2's `Redo` is a BUTTON, and the cluster is seven.** §16x had `Redo` as a `More` row under `⇧⌘Z`; the tree gives it the slot beside `Undo`, because the two halves of one gesture belong in one place and a row you have to go and find is not that place. Both stand disabled rather than vanishing (rule 13: a cluster that changes shape as a stack fills is one nobody can design for), `More` is then `Clear…` · `Delete…`, and the arithmetic the floor rests on is 16 + 7×18 + 6×4 + 4 = 170px inside 235 — the frame `c2-draw-small` is still the evidence and now shows seven slots.

The armed tool's glyph on the BUTTON stays — one fact, two jobs (what a press will make, and which row makes it), not one fact in two slots.
- 2026-09-12 · **The drawing's cluster answers focus and `[data-hot]`, never a bare hover.** §16x's carve-out had no line of CSS behind it: `canvas.css`'s general `.blk:hover` revealed a drawing's picker on every incidental pass over a sheet a person is inking. `drawing.css` now takes it back for `.blk-draw` alone.
- 2026-09-12 · **The ink ladder is the accent at three ALPHAS (100 · 78 · 56), not three mixes toward the paper.** The chart's ladder tints toward `--bg-panel` and reads, because a bar is 40px wide; ink is a 1px line and 13px words, and at 45%/22% toward a light panel the second step's label faded into the paper and the third was a fill colour pretending to be a mark. Alpha keeps the hue at every step. The two ladders are now different on purpose and each says why in its own file. Residual, recorded rather than hidden: measured against the actual tokens (WCAG relative luminance, `--accent` #2f6bff over `--bg-panel` #ffffff / `--bg-raised` #f1f3f7 in light, #5b8cff over #1e2128 in dark), the third step (56% alpha) lands at 2.22:1 on the white panel and 2.12:1 on the raised paper an empty sheet shows — both below the 3:1 floor for a hairline; the second step (78%) clears it at 3.15:1 / 2.94:1. The dark-theme third step reads better, at 2.53:1, which is the number an earlier draft of this line gave for light. Holding the Text tool and the Hairline weight to steps 0–1 was refused because an ink menu that changes shape by tool is a control that changes shape (rule 13's own reasoning), so the step is documented as de-emphasis and the frames `c2-draw*-light` carry one label per step as the evidence.

A result or a note's pill still aims nothing: a block that sends its own content in the TAGGED block needs no canvas target to be answered, and adding one would be a destination nobody asked for (§16aa, open).

A render that fails is all-or-nothing: the cue says so and the route falls to `"none"`, so no line ever promises an attachment the message does not carry.
