- 2026-09-12 · **The claude -p round trip is MEASURED, not assumed** (maintainer call 5). One real exchange (`scratchpad/c2b-vision-probe.log`, the session's one network call) sent a scratch canvas holding one drawing — an ellipse and an arrow pointing at it, no text label — through the app's own `claude -p` adapter via `canvas_read`. The image arrived (44.2KB base64, content block first); the model answered "a blue circle on the right and a blue arrow pointing diagonally upward to the right toward it," naming shapes present nowhere in the 38 characters of text it was given. 2 turns, 5,203ms wall, `is_error` false. No `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS` message and no truncation on either stream — the child's own default budget carries a 44KB reply whole (input 3,866 tokens, output 187, $0.00578); this narrows "not verified" to "the child's default carries a real drawing," not to "5MB passes" — `TOOL_IMAGE_B64_MAX` stands as a generous ceiling, not a tested one, though the renderer's own 1568px clamp means a drawing cannot reach anywhere near it. The app's own rmcp server was not in the loop (a ~40-line stand-in server was, both ends of the wire it stands in for separately verified in rmcp's own source); one model, one exchange — Opus/Sonnet are flagged `vision: true` from the same family documentation and are untested here.
- 2026-09-12 · **Final gate: nine places where the law described chrome or mechanics the tree did not ship, found and closed, no code moved.** AGENT-UX §16x still read "six hot," `Redo` folded into `More` under `⇧⌘Z` — the tree (and this file's own fix-round entry, above) has shipped seven hot with `Redo` as its own button since the fix round; §16x is corrected to match, with the `170px inside 235px` arithmetic. §16w named an unbuilt `⇧` square/circle/line modifier, an `autoFit` identifier that does not exist (the path is `resizeTo(…, { auto: true })` off `minSpanFor`), `overflow: auto` where `drawing.css` ships `overflow: visible` on purpose (LESSONS 7, the opposite call), a pen stroke "sealing into a second stroke" where it simply seals and stops, and a document-level 256KB cap that was never built (the shipped cap is two ceilings, not three). §16y said `BlockRef` gains pixels (`image?: {mime,data,w,h}`) where the tree carries `drawing?: true`/`canvasId?: string` and no pixels, and that the model's picture is "the SAME render `Copy` produces" where `Copy` puts SVG on the clipboard and the model is sent a separate raster (`drawingImage`'s `toPng`) — two sentences of the same section contradicting each other. All nine are corrected in AGENT-UX.md by this pass; the ink ladder's residual contrast figure ("2.6:1") and `mentions.ts`'s cause-blind `"none"`-route sentence are real but non-blocking (S3) and are carried to ROADMAP_log's open list rather than fixed in code this pass.

### Agent D1 · the maintainer's ten findings (2026-09-14; screenshots of the live build, law in AGENT-UX.md §2/§13.6/§16l/§16p, AGENT-SPEC.md §5.1/§9)

- 2026-09-14 · **Insert from the pane APPENDS; the caret keeps its own gesture.** `editorInsert` gained a second argument rather than a second seam (`InsertWhere = "caret" | "end"`, the caret still the default), so the sidebar's column double-click is byte-for-byte what it was and every Insert in the pane goes through `ResultBlock.insertSql(text, "end")` alone. The append leaves exactly **one blank line** (`APPEND_BLANK_LINES = 2` newlines, minus whatever the buffer already ends in, none at all when it is empty) and lands the caret on the statement's FIRST character, not its end, with `scrollIntoView`: the thing just inserted is the thing to be looking at (LESSONS 8). `appendInsert(len, tail, text)` is a pure function over the buffer's last two characters, so the arithmetic is tested without a CodeMirror.
- 2026-09-14 · **The prose and the result block stand 16px apart, and nothing in the column measured 0.** The finding said "fix the one that is 0"; every pairing in `.ans-tail` was already the column's 8, and a screenshot reads 8px between a line box's bottom and a grid's header as none. The 8 stays the column's gap and the extra 8 is the BLOCK's own margin (`.ans-prose + .rb`, `+ .rb-head`): the headline, the block and the `rows · ms` line are one unit at 8 inside, so the 16 lands above whichever of the two stands first. An empty prose is `display: contents` and takes no margin at all, so a streaming run whose result lands before its first word still opens no gap over nothing (DESIGN rule 2).
- 2026-09-14 · **The ordinal's gutter is 20px, right-aligned, and the marker is absolutely positioned — never a flex cell.** The UA's own `::marker` lays out to the LEFT of the content edge, so `70.` (18.5px) hung 6px past the answer's left margin while `1.` (9.4px) sat inside it: two indents in one list. `li::before` in a fixed 20px gutter with `tabular-nums` ends both on one edge; a bullet's cell is 12px, which lands its text column on the same 16px the UA gave it, so no bulleted answer moved. The first cut used a flex row per item and the frame caught it — a flex container turns every `.fig`, `<b>` and `<code>` in the line into its own flex item, and one sentence broke into five boxes on two lines (LESSONS 8: geometry bugs are proved in pixels).
- 2026-09-14 · **A lead-in keeps its 4px; 16 everywhere would have broken what a lead-in IS.** The finding asked for "the same 16px above and below a list", and a label directly above the group it names belongs to the group (AGENT-UX §2 item 3). One exception, stated in the CSS beside the rule it excepts.
- 2026-09-14 · **The composer backdrop's clip widened from 2px to 4px, exactly where B2 predicted the fix would be.** A pill at position 0 outdents 2px and its RING stands a further 1px outside that, so a clip at 2px cut the left edge off while the fill and the icon stood whole (measured at the 320 floor: the pill's box at x=48 against the clip at 48, the ring at 47). The wrap pads `--sp-1` on both sides and takes it straight back in margin, the backdrop pays the same 4 back as its own padding: the textarea's content box does not move, only the clip edge, which now stands 1px clear of the widest thing a pill paints.
- 2026-09-14 · **A reload tells a committed run from a rolled-back one, with no migration.** The finding named `agent_turn_update` on the TEXT column; that command patches `agent_turns`, which has no status column, so the write goes through the answer row's one writer, `agent_answer_put` (AGENT-SPEC §9) — same column, same row, no schema change. `agent_answers.status` for a change is now `proposed | ran | committed | rolled_back`. The persisted spelling `rolled_back` is deliberately not `ranTx`'s in-memory `rolledback`, and exactly ONE map translates (`TX_STATUS` / `txFromStatus`), so the two can never drift through a second, uncoordinated conversion (LESSONS 11). `AnswerStatus` does NOT gain the two: they never stand on `Exchange.status`, which keeps saying `ran`.
- 2026-09-14 · **Every widget clips its content, and the clip rides the DRAG layer, not the cell frame.** `.cvg-drag { overflow: hidden }` is the page's ONE clipping rule: one box further in would cut the lift's own shadow and the 2% it grows by. A chart drawing 432px of plot into a 292px face used to land 128px of it on the note below, which no arithmetic inside the face could have stopped.
- 2026-09-14 · **A squeezed chart draws the rows that FIT and says how many on the block's own status line.** The face hands `{shown, total}` up (`Chart.onFit`, one layout effect, the two numbers as the dependency) and `statusOf(block, bars)` prints `7 of 12 bars · 214.7 ms` where `12 rows · 214.7 ms` stood, exactly as `mismatch` rides the same line. Built first as a second line inside the face (`.cv-chart-cap`, 20px reserved out of the plot) and changed when the review named it: a row of a bar chart IS a bar, so that line put the same twelve in two slots and added one more always-visible line to a page whose count of them is the thing C2a spent itself on (DESIGN rules 14 and 15). Nothing is reserved inside the face any more, so a squeezed plot gets the whole of its box. One scale across every row, drawn or not: rescaling to the rows that fit would make the bars lie about each other where the line tells the truth.
- 2026-09-14 · **A chart OPENS at a height read from its bars and its series, capped at six rows.** `chartPx(spec, lines)` reads the same three numbers `barLayout` does (`ns` bars a row, a legend from two series, `PITCH_MAX`); the engine's own `defaultSpan` sized a one-series chart and nothing else, which is why a three-series chart opened three rows tall and drew twelve. The cap is the note's own ceiling (`CHART_ROWS_MAX = 6`): past it a 40-bar reading would open a widget eleven rows tall, so it opens at six and says `N of 40 bars`. One constant if a later wave wants a different ceiling.
- 2026-09-14 · **The sheet paints paper with ink on it or without, and the corner handle leaves with the cluster it belongs to.** C2b painted `--bg-raised` only while a drawing was empty, on the reasoning that ink is its own surface; a stroke and a lone corner glyph standing on the page with nothing around them is what that looked like. `data-empty` is gone from `Drawing.tsx` entirely (one rule, no state to get wrong), the empty-state frame is unchanged because the empty case always painted it, and the drawing is the one kind whose grid handle is suppressed on a bare hover, since its cluster does not answer one either. The block cluster on the grid now sits ON the box (`top: 0`) rather than floating 2px above it, where the new clip would have cut it.
- 2026-09-14 · **The note fills its box, width and height, and scrolls inside it.** The note was the grid's one exception on the grounds that its words set its height; they still do — that is what picks the cells it OPENS at — but a hand that then gives it 6×3 gave it a box, and `height: 100%` resolved against the entrance wrapper's `auto` is zero, which is what left a note 18px tall in a 108px widget. The cells now reach the block through the wrapper (`.cvg-slot:not(.cvg-draft) .cv-item`). The words stay anchored at the top in the prose register — the type is not scaled up to fill 6×3 — and whether a box should GROW to fit its words instead of scrolling is left open on purpose.
- 2026-09-14 · **A refused compare is one fragment of the status line the block already had, and never a face change.** `mismatch` rides the block in SESSION state and `writeDoc` drops it at the door, so a reload never restates a refusal whose cause may since have been fixed (LESSONS 5). `compareMismatch(name, raw)` reads the object out of Postgres's own sentence for the two plain cases (`relation "x" does not exist` → `table x is not on prod-crawler`; the column case likewise) and otherwise keeps the driver's own first line prefixed with the side that said it — inventing a friendlier sentence for an error nobody here has read is how a status line starts lying (LESSONS 9). The diff face's cells ellipsize and its grid scrolls inside the widget, so no compare can stretch it.
- 2026-09-14 · **`[object Object]` could not be reproduced at HEAD and the directive was implemented literally anyway.** No diff cell carried a `title` at all and the chart face renders no tooltip element; one formatter now prints the A · B · Δ triple as the cell's tooltip, and the compared block was scanned to confirm nothing on it says `[object`. The exact element the maintainer hovered is unconfirmed — recorded rather than claimed fixed (LESSONS 9).
- 2026-09-14 · **`canvas_create({ title })` is the model's own door, gated on the question's word AND the platform's.** `canMake = !!req.canvasNew && (!!canvas || saysCanvas(req.question))`: the platform half makes EVAL §4's byte-identical five structural rather than dependent on what a bench question happens to say (the eval passes no maker at all). The predicate is `saysCanvas`, not `mentionsCanvas` — in this codebase a *mention* is the `@` ladder and a canvas mention is AGENT-UX §16l route 1, the opposite of the word route — and it is one regex in one home, imported by `stores/agent.ts` so the app's door and the model's read the same test (DESIGN rule 14).
- 2026-09-14 · **One widening of AGENT-SPEC §5.1's "6", cited at the call site (DESIGN rule 15): a provider that owns its loop carries the three writers from the start.** `claude -p` mints its MCP token ONCE per exchange, so a 6-tool array would let the child open a canvas it could then never write a block into, and item 10 would stay unfixed on the app's DEFAULT provider. On that path alone the three ride from turn one and refuse in `CANVAS_NOT_MADE`'s one sentence until the create lands; HTTP providers are offered 5, 6 or 9 exactly as counted. `canvas_create` is the canvas family's LAST name in file order, which keeps B3's measured 8-tool prefix and the Rust "five first, then `canvasTools` in file order" pin intact.
- 2026-09-14 · **The title cap is `CANVAS_NAME_CAP` (tools.ts, 80), not the store's `TITLE_CAP`** — nothing under `src/agent` may import a store. Same number, and the JSON schema's `maxLength` is pinned equal to it.
- 2026-09-14 · **A second `canvas_create` in one exchange makes a SECOND canvas and re-targets again**; the turn cap and the offer itself (only a target or the question's own word shows the tool) are what bound a model that keeps asking. Built first as a refusal, on the ground that a loop which can open tabs can bury a user in them, and changed when the case it would refuse turned out to be item 10's own: a connection that already holds a canvas and a question asking for a NEW one, answered with `this answer already has a canvas`. §5.1's refusal paragraph and the tool's schema description both said the opposite of the tree until D1's fix round; the earlier citation of "§5.1's last-write-wins sentence" was wrong (that sentence is about bridge registrations keyed on session id, §5.1's own wire paragraph, not about a second create) and is withdrawn here.
- 2026-09-14 · **The law's own account of finding 10a was wrong about the tree, and is corrected rather than repeated.** `aimCanvas` has ALWAYS looked for `kind === "canvas"` alone; it was AGENT-UX §16l item 4's prose that read "no pill already stands in the draft". What actually silenced the maintainer's example was item 4's third clause, a connection that already holds a canvas tab — which is precisely why `canvas_create` exists. A unit test now pins a table-pilled question through the word route regardless.
- 2026-09-14 · **Final gate over the whole D1 wave: every citation above re-read against the shipped tree, cold, nothing found past what the evening fix round had already closed.** Checked line by line: `appendInsert`/`InsertWhere`/`APPEND_BLANK_LINES` in `SqlEditor.tsx` and the single `ResultBlock.insertSql(text, "end")` call site (item 1); `.ans-tail`'s `--sp-2` flex gap plus `.ans-prose + .rb`/`.rb-head`'s own `--sp-2` margin summing to the stated 16px, and `.ans-text > .ans-grid`'s direct `--sp-4` for the model's own grid inside prose — the second fact item 2's entry above states in words ("the same 16 between a block and a following text") without naming the selector; named here so the rule and the sentence match token for token (item 2); the li-gutter arithmetic and the lead-in's kept 4px (item 5); the composer clip's `--sp-1`-to-`--sp-1` widen and the taste-gate chip rule's ring-not-fill line (item 3); `TX_STATUS`/`txFromStatus`/`stampTxEnd`/`persistWriteStatus` as the one writer through `agent_answer_put` (item 4); `.cvg-drag{overflow:hidden}` as the page's one clip, `barLayout`'s `{shown,total}` and `chartPx`'s series-aware default (item 6); `data-empty`'s removal from `Drawing.tsx` and the hover-suppressed handle selector (item 7); `.note-body`'s `flex:1 1 0`/`overflow:auto` reached through the entrance wrapper (item 8); `triText`/`compareMismatch` and the diff grid's `minmax(0,max-content)` scroll (item 9); `CANVAS_TOOL_NAMES` four-wide in both `tools.ts` and `agent_canvas.rs`, `CanvasMakeName`, and `canMake`'s gate in `stores/agent.ts` (item 10). AGENT-SPEC's own "Open (D1, 2026-09-14)" paragraph (a Restart-cut orphans a `canvas_create`d tab) and AGENT-UX's note-fill grow-vs-scroll deferral both still stand as written, correctly left open rather than guessed shut. Gates run fresh from a clean shell rather than trusted from the fix round's own log: `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean, `bun scripts/design-lint.ts` 0 finding(s) (census mode), `bun test` 1,275 pass / 0 fail (72 files, 66,018 `expect()` calls — the exact count the fix round's own log carries, the tree unmoved since), `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean, `cargo test` unchanged at 136 pass / 0 fail (37 ignored, live-DB). No code moved by this pass; the one non-blocking S3 (a note's overflow has no scroll affordance at rest, `grid.css:193`) is re-verified present and stays ledgered in `ROADMAP_log.md`'s Open list rather than fixed here, per that list's own stated deferral to D2.

### Agent D2 · widgets and the answer's rhythm (2026-09-14; the maintainer's twelve answers, picture: the "D2 · widgets and the answer's rhythm" section of ask-sketch-d2.html; law in AGENT-UX.md §2/§2b/§2c/§16bb–§16hh, AGENT-SPEC.md §6/§9, EVAL.md §4)

- 2026-09-14 · **A widget wears one frame, and only one.** A3 counted 0 frames per widget and the maintainer reversed it by name, so the count is amended in the law rather than quietly broken (DESIGN rule 15): the hairline lives on `.cvg-drag`, the clip layer, so the line stands outside the padding box the clip cuts and the lift's 2% scale and `--shadow-pop` carry it. Everything inside that gives the widget a SECOND edge gives it up instead — the table's own band bleeds to the widget's left and right lines, the diff's grid likewise, the drawing's paper IS the padding box, and the note's edit ring is an inset box-shadow rather than a border, since a border would sit exactly where the clip cuts. The page of four went from 1 frame (the table's own) to 4, and always-visible controls per widget stayed 0.
- 2026-09-14 · **The canvas gains one strip, and it is title + one icon button, nothing else** (DESIGN rule 12). The name is DATA and is drawn as typed, never Title Cased; a click makes it an input in one geometry (§16j) and the store's one `rename` carries the tab label with it. The `+` opens a menu of KINDS, which is the reason §16b's "menu rows carry a label, a hint and an arrow, never an icon" is amended rather than broken: a menu of ACTIONS on one object stays bare (`More`), a menu that hands you a shape or a widget wears its glyph (`Pen ▾`, `+`). The column it wears is MenuNode's existing `glyph?`, not a second `icon?` field — two spellings of one concept is WRITING rule 5's own case.
- 2026-09-14 · **The note follows its words until a hand takes its corner, and then never again.** `autoH` (AGENT-SPEC §9's own field, which needed no new flag) is the document's record of who owns the height: while it stands, the surface re-runs §16p's height formula against the WORDS on every input and steps the span by whole rows through `resize()`, so the neighbours make room on `spring.layout`; it never shrinks mid-edit, because a page must not jump under the caret, and the commit re-measures once and takes any shrink then. The first hand resize clears it for good and the words scroll under a 16px foot fade — which closes D1's ledgered S3 (a note's overflow had no scroll affordance at rest).
- 2026-09-14 · **The count of bars a squeezed chart could not draw moved INTO the face, as the one line that acts on it.** `+ N more` is the link-button species (`.linkish`, DESIGN rule 1) standing at the bars' own `x0` where the next bar would; a press resizes the widget to `defaultSpanFor`'s own height — the same arithmetic that chose the opening one — on `spring.layout`, its WIDTH untouched because a hand chose that. D1's `8 of 12 bars` status fragment is retired for it (DESIGN rule 14: the count now lives in the line that acts on it), `statusOf` loses its `bars` parameter, `StatusLine` loses `sides`, `barsShown` is deleted, and `Chart.onFit` / `ResultBlock.onChartFit` / the `BarsFit` prop type went with the seam.
- 2026-09-14 · **A comparison's two connections are chips inside the diff face, not names in the status line.** Each chip carries that connection's own 8px identity dot (rule 5's one listed exception, the provenance mark), its name at tier 1 and that SIDE's own timing at tier 2; the compared chip trails the `×` that clears the compare and the origin chip carries none, since clearing leaves the first side standing exactly where it is. The status line drops both names and both timings and reads `6 rows · assumed Last Month = August 2026` (rule 14 again). They are the tool chip's costume (`.tchip`), not the bordered `.chip`: a chip that answers no click must not wear the toggle species. A refused compare shows no chips — nothing stood.
- 2026-09-14 · **A long list folds after twelve behind one `Show All N`, one-way.** The reader asked for the list, so there is no way back for the exchange; the line names the WHOLE list, not the hidden tail, and the parser that folds it is the same one a canvas note renders through, so a note whose words are a list folds too. It carries no `aria-expanded` — the collapsed state it would announce never exists again after the press — and the list's honesty is `role="list"`: twelve items and a button under them, never a seventy that hides fifty-eight.
- 2026-09-14 · **PROMPT_VERSION v5 was measured and REVERTED; item 6 ships as the fold alone.** One sentence in `HYBRID_RULES` asking that a list of identifiers come back as a query's result cost `pagila-insight.json` + claude-haiku-4-5 its presentation row (0.850 → 0.771, re-sampled 0.750, slack 0.05) while accuracy held everywhere. A v4 control run the same hour on the same machine read 0.857, so the loss is attributable to the sentence and not to the model, the CLI or the machine — the practice is recommended in EVAL §4 for any prompt wave whose baseline row is older than the tooling. The check that moved in both draws is `no_grid_restatement`: a sentence pushing identifiers INTO the result made the model read more of the result back in prose, the same trade v3 made in the other direction. `PROMPT_VERSION` stays `v4`, `eval/baseline.json` does not move, and `prompt.ts` gained nine comment lines so the next wave does not retry the sentence blind.
- 2026-09-14 · **A grid element is a WIDGET in every string a reader sees, and a `block` in every identifier.** `canvasStatusText` reads `4 widgets · Canvas 4` / `1 widget` / `3 widgets · 1 replaced`, `restartConfirmText` reads `N canvas widgets`, and the comments quoting those strings moved with them. Model-facing bytes did NOT move — `prompt.ts`, `tools.ts`, `canvas.tauri.ts` and `mentions.ts` still say `block` to the model — because every one of those is a prompt byte under EVAL §4's own gate, and a naming wave does not get to re-baseline the benches. AGENT-UX's ~230 historical uses in prose are named as open work (§16gg) rather than renamed halfway.
- 2026-09-14 · **The OpenAI and Gemini registry rows read `vision: true` as a FAMILY default, off documentation this wave actually read; DeepSeek, Mistral, Groq and xAI stay `unknown`.** 7 true / 2 false / 8 unknown. The two tests and the one fixture that used `gpt-5.6-terra` AS an unknown row were swapped to `mistral/mistral-large-3` rather than deleted: the Settings vision switch is the app's one conditional row (§16y) and a frame of it needs a model that genuinely stays unknown, or the frame silently becomes a picture of nothing.
- 2026-09-14 · **The strip's 40px is a cost every canvas card pays, and it was measured, not derived.** Four older states (`c2-draw`, `c2-draw-tools`, `c2-draw-novision`, `c2-draw-empty`) are read WHOLE by their own fixtures' rule and began cropping their documents by exactly the strip's height while `tsc` and every unit test stayed green; C2a's two layout states lost another 120 to the values row's second cell. Every canvas card is now the number a CDP measurement of `.cv-scroll` returned at 640 · 960 · 1280 (0px of overflow on every whole-read state), and both copies of each number — the fixture's and `ask-frames.ts`'s — moved together.
- 2026-09-14 · **A values widget opens and floors at TWO cell rows.** Item 1's 12px band costs every widget 24px of height and a figure row cleared a single cell by two pixels, which is why C2a's own evidence frame was drawing its captions through its status line. `defaultSpanFor`/`minSpanFor` gained a `valueRows()` with the pixels named (`VALUES_ROW_PX`, `VALUES_CHROME`), the way `chartPx` already names the chart's. A document written before this wave keeps the cells the hand gave it and clips, which is D1's own rule; a migration would be one clamp in `laidOut` if the maintainer wants one.

### Agent · D2 (the maintainer's answers, 2026-09-14; picture: the D2 rows of ask-sketch-d2.html; answers: qwry-agent-lab/docs/questions/answers.md)

- 2026-09-14 · **Final gate over the whole D2 wave: every seat's citation re-read against the shipped tree, cold, and one drift found and fixed.** The item-by-item record already stands above under "Agent D2 · widgets and the answer's rhythm," written by the wave's own seats before this pass; this entry is the confirmation, not a restatement, per the same rule the D1 final gate used (LESSONS 5/9: a wave's own plan is not evidence that the plan happened). All of it matches its law citation. `PROMPT_VERSION` is confirmed `"v4"` in `prompt.ts`, and `HYBRID_RULES` carries no trace of the measured-and-reverted v5 sentence.
- 2026-09-14 · **The one drift: a fixture's own caption described a document the seed no longer builds.** `src/harness/fixtures.d2canvas.ts`'s header called `d2-note-grown` "three rows of cells for three rows of words" while the `grown` block it seeds is `h: 2` and the re-shot stills (`d2-note-grown-{640,1280}-light`) measure exactly two rows (227.5 CSS px = 2 × `CELL_H` + `GUTTER`). The caption is reworded to "two rows of cells for the words it holds," which states what the seed and the frame agree on rather than a number left over from an earlier draft of the seed. Nothing that renders moved; no frame was re-shot for it.
- 2026-09-14 · **Every reversal this wave makes, named in one place.** A3's "0 frames per widget" → 1, by the maintainer's own call (item 1, AGENT-UX §16bb). AGENT-UX §16f's "zero strips of its own" for the canvas → 1 strip, title + `+` (item 2, §16cc). §16b's "menu rows carry a label, a hint and an arrow, never an icon" → "a menu of ACTIONS is bare, a menu of KINDS wears its glyph," with the C2b picker's own "deliberate exception" framing folded into this rule's first instance rather than staying an exception beside it (item 2, §16cc; DESIGN's Menu-row entry amended to match). D1's `8 of 12 bars` status-line fragment → retired, the count moved into the chart face's own `+ N more` line (item 4, §16ee, DESIGN rule 14 applied to itself). ### Agent · D3 (the maintainer's drag recording, 2026-09-15; evidence: the screen recording's frames f006 to f054; law: AGENT-UX §16q, §16s, §16t)

- 2026-09-15 · **A gesture moves only what it touches, and C2a's "push down, then float up" is retired as `move()`'s and `resize()`'s rule.** Both now pin the held element where the hand left it and hand the layout to `pushDown()` (`src/canvas/grid.ts`), which drops every element the pinned one intersects to the first row that clears its bottom edge, transitively for whatever those then intersect, in `(y, x, id)` order, and moves nothing else; the walk drains because nothing is enqueued that did not just travel strictly down and `ROW_MAX` is the ceiling. The maintainer's recording is the evidence and the reason a rule D2 had just reaffirmed is replaced rather than kept: lifting the note "Hi" (3x2, top-left) and dragging it down slid an unrelated note at the lower-right UPWARD though the two never intersect, because `move()` handed the WHOLE layout to `compact()` with only the dragged element pinned. So a hole a gesture opens now stands ABOVE an element as it already could beside one, and the C2a line above ("a drop is PINNED through the compaction") is superseded here: the drop is pinned against compaction full stop, because `move()` calls no compaction. Float-up itself is not deleted, it stops being a gesture's: `compact()` keeps its float-up for its one live caller, the store's `compacted()` on a delete or a doomed-agent cut (the page rearranging itself, AGENT-UX §16g's "the gap it leaves closes"), and `firstFit()` keeps its row-major scan for `place()` seating a new widget and `reflow()` re-laying the layout at a lower column count. Whether a delete should still close every hole now that a gesture's holes stand is put to the maintainer in the ROADMAP_log note, not decided here: §16g is pre-existing law and outside this wave's rule. The engine's invariants are unmoved (no overlap, inside the columns, deterministic, bounds down-only) and the property tests moved to the new rule, plus one asserting that a widget off the dragged one's path keeps its cell exactly through a move and through a resize.
- 2026-09-15 · **The preview IS the commit, and a drop plays ONE spring.** What the placeholder and the pushed neighbours show mid-drag is byte-for-byte the layout `move()`/`resize()` writes on release, asserted by feeding one pointer path to both, so a neighbour standing in its pushed place when the pointer lifts does not move again and only the held element animates. The landing rides the commit rather than the pointer handler: `commit()` carries a `Map<id, Drop>` of what each moved element has left to do (two zeroes for a neighbour, the pointer's own residual offset for the held one) and the slot applies it in the SAME frame it applies the new cell frame, which is written once with no transition of its own; nothing is written to a handle on pointerup and no second `setDoc` or re-layout follows the spring. Written the other way round, handles on pointerup and cells one render later, the element painted at the place it came from and then sprang, which is the jump back and forth the recording shows. Two smaller facts fell out of the same bug: an offset is a PLACE and is written with `jump`, never `set`, or the drag's last two pointer frames hand the settle the velocity of the whole travel and it overshoots its cell by more than a row and comes back; and the gesture clears itself BEFORE the commit, because a surface still wearing the gesture's mark read the synchronous render as one made mid-drag and threw the landing away. Reduced motion collapses the preset and the widget lands at once. New harness states: `d3-drag-nonintersecting` and `d3-drop-settled`, in a new `src/harness/fixtures.d3.ts`.

### Agent A3 · D4 (one layout: the derived split removed, 2026-09-15; evidence: the maintainer's second recording and the probe at 660/960/1280)

- 2026-09-15 · **The diagnosis is real and confirmed against the pre-fix tree, file:line, but it is not the maintainer's failure.** On `HEAD` (340fdb4) `src/stores/canvas.ts` held a module-scope `const native = new Map<string, {columns, blocks}>()` (`:1893`), written by `reflowTo` whenever the page was narrower than the layout's own right edge (`persist: false` writes at `:1673` and `:1679`, `native.delete` at `:1651`/`:1857`) and read back by `columnsOfDoc`; `CanvasGrid.tsx` marked the render `flowing` (`:758`) and gated a synchronous commit on `reflowing()` (`:623`). That is a second, session-lived layout beside the document's own, and it did what DESIGN rule 14 says a fact stored twice will do: a window round trip on the pre-fix tree returns the wide arrangement whole (`d4-short` back at `[3,0,3,1]` after 1280 → 660 → 1280, `d4-probe-head-middrag.log`). But every one of the four original gesture cases (drop on empty ground, drop on a neighbour, resize on a neighbour, keyboard move) at 660/960/1280 PASSED on that same pre-fix tree — **75 PASS / 0 FAIL**, verified this pass by re-reading `d4-probe-head.log` directly rather than trusting the count handed up: a persisted, `user: true` commit ran `native.delete` before any later frame could re-derive from the cache, so no gesture commit was ever thrown away at any width. And the maintainer's own frames measure a card wide enough for ten columns, a NATIVE count with no derived layout in play (AGENT-UX §16q's pixel accounting off f095/f110). The mechanism that actually threw his resize and his drop away stays open (ROADMAP_log, D4) — this entry states that rather than let §16q's citation stand alone.
- 2026-09-15 · **The rule is shipped anyway, DESIGN rule 14 and the orchestrator's call rather than a fix for an unreproduced bug.** Confirmed against the tree as it now stands: `reflowTo` (`src/stores/canvas.ts:1656-1687`) measures the narrowing against `rightEdgeOf(laid)`, never `lastColumns`; at or past that edge it only updates the in-memory, unpersisted `lastColumns` advisory and returns (no second layout is read or written); short of it, `reflow()` runs once and the result is written through the same `setDoc` every gesture commits through (`persist` defaults true), its moved widgets travelling on `spring.layout`. The `native` map and every one of its four call sites are gone (`grep -n native src/stores/canvas.ts` returns nothing); `setColumns` is deleted. **The trade-off is stated, not hidden**: a widening back does not restore the wider arrangement, because no second copy survives to restore it from — reading order is what a reflow keeps (the round-trip property test), not position — and both `reflowTo`'s own doc comment and AGENT-UX §16q say so in those words. `lastColumns` now means only the count the layout was last COMMITTED at; a window that merely opens a document still raises it in memory (so a model write or a delete's compaction places at the count the reader is looking at) but persists nothing (LESSONS 5).
- 2026-09-15 · **A commit landing mid-gesture no longer draws the held widget away from its own placeholder, and the landing offset is read from the frame the gesture drew rather than the one it was pressed on.** `rebase()` (`CanvasGrid.tsx:487-519`): when another id's cell moved under a held gesture it re-answers (`owe(g)`) rather than leaving the drag layer's math stale; when the held element's OWN cell moves mid-lift, the frame's travel is absorbed into the drag layer's offset (`driftX`/`driftY`, jumped, never sprung) so the box under the pointer does not jump. `dropOf` (`:313-330`) takes `at`, read in `up()` (`:541`) from `cfg.items()` — the cell the element is rendered at right now — before `clear()` runs, in place of the cell it was pressed on (`g.base`), closing the half of the maintainer's f110 this wave CAN reproduce: a commit mid-gesture drawing the held widget away from its placeholder. Verified in the test file rather than taken on the comment: `CanvasGrid.test.tsx` calls `s.grab.rebase("a")`/`rebase("b")`/`rebase()` across cases that move another id's cell, the held id's own cell, and a pitch-only change.
- 2026-09-15 · **Nothing paints at a guessed column count, which is what lets the derived/native split's render mark (`flowing`/`reflowing`) be deleted with it.** `CanvasGrid.tsx` starts `columns` at 0 and renders the grid host with no slots until the layout effect measures the real width, so a narrow open never shows a layout at a count wider than the page and then slides — confirmed: `flowing`/`reflowing` (formerly `:758`/`:623`) are gone from the file (`grep -n flowing\|reflowing src/canvas/CanvasGrid.tsx` returns nothing).
- 2026-09-15 · **Probe numbers, re-verified this pass rather than copied from the wave's own log.** `d4-probe-head.log`: 75 PASS / 0 FAIL (pre-fix tree, four gestures × three widths, the refutation above). `d4-probe-after.log`: **97 PASS / 0 FAIL**, ending `ALL PASS` — five gestures (the fourth original set plus a card narrowing while the widget is held) at 660/960/1280 plus the window round trip, which now reads `cells at 660 {"d4-short":[0,4,3,1]}` → `cells back {"d4-short":[0,4,3,1]}` (the flow stands, where the pre-fix tree restored `[3,0,3,1]`). The reviewer's own independent driver (`scratchpad/d4-attack/`, three documents × five widths × eight gestures plus width sweeps) read 6 failures before this round's fixes (`attack-before-fix.log:92`) and **0 failure(s), 0 note(s)** after (`run-after-fix.log`, tail confirmed this pass).
- 2026-09-15 · **One review finding is confirmed already resolved rather than re-fixed: the evidence-citation drift.** A review pass had flagged that this wave's `ROADMAP_log` entry risked citing the superseded `d4-probe.log` (79 PASS, recorded while a 1280 reflow still misread `columns 7 -> 10`, before the fixer round closed the `lastColumns`-stuck-at-the-old-count defect) as its evidence rather than the final run. Checked directly this pass: the entry below cites `d4-probe-after.log` throughout (97 PASS / 0 FAIL), and this entry's own probe bullet cites `d4-probe-head.log` (75 PASS / 0 FAIL) for the pre-fix baseline — neither cites the superseded file. No edit was needed; recorded here so the finding does not stand as unresolved.
- 2026-09-15 · **One S3 finding from review is carried open, not fixed this pass (out of this seat's scope: one code seat owns the engine files this wave).** `rebase()` (`CanvasGrid.tsx:499-500`) returns before calling `owe()` when the held element's frame ORIGIN is unchanged in px — true for any element at column 0 (`x = cell.x * (m.cellW + m.gutter)` is 0 regardless of the new pitch) or row 0 under a pitch-only change (gutter/cellW changing without the column count changing), so the drag preview can go stale against the new pitch until the next actual pointer move recomputes it fresh. Reproduces only in the narrow window between a mid-drag resize event and the next pointermove, for a held element at `x=0` or `y=0`; does not affect the landing (`up()` always reads fresh metrics) or any of the twelve probe cases (none holds an element at the origin while narrowing). Fix shape: call `owe(g)` on any `id === undefined` rebase where `g.moved`, not only when the anchor's px moved, or compare the pitch itself rather than the derived px. Left for the next wave to pick up alongside the maintainer's still-unreproduced recording.

### Agent A3 · D5 (a dev-only trace for the unreproduced gesture bug, 2026-09-15)

- 2026-09-15 · **The instrument is one branch, and the branch is `import.meta.env.DEV` at every call site, not only inside the recorder.** `src/canvas/trace.ts` holds a 2000-entry ring and a `trace(kind, fields)` that returns before touching it unless this is a dev build; every seam that calls it (`CanvasGrid.tsx` measure / gesture / land / autoH, `stores/canvas.ts` commit / persist) is itself written `if (import.meta.env.DEV) trace(...)`, so Vite folds the literal and the argument objects never reach a release frame. Measured rather than asserted: `dist/assets` is **1,821,023 → 1,821,107 bytes** (46 files, +84), every `index-*` chunk byte-identical, and no dev string (`__qwryCanvasTrace`, `Copy Canvas Trace`) is greppable in `dist/`. The +84 is the one thing that is NOT dead in release: the `cause` label each layout door hands `setDoc` (`"move"`, `"resize"`, `"growTo/autoH"`, `"reflow"`, `"model"`), kept as an honest parameter rather than hidden in module state that a reader would have to chase.
- 2026-09-15 · **A commit's `gesture` field means "a hand was down when this landed", not "this commit is the drop".** The gesture machine clears itself before `cfg.commit` runs (D3 rule 3, so a synchronous render never reads the gesture's own mark), so the drop's own commit records `gesture: null` and is named by its `cause` instead. What the field is for is the other case: a reflow, a note measuring its words, or a model write landing WHILE an element is held, which is the half of the maintainer's recording D4 could reproduce and the shape the rest of it is suspected to have.

### Agent · E1 (session identity: one resolver, death clears the stamp, heal re-stamps, 2026-09-16; evidence: the maintainer's strip under a green dot)

- 2026-09-16 · **The diagnosis, confirmed against the tree rather than assumed from the brief.** `state.rs:47`'s `AppState::session` returns `None` for an id no longer in `AppState.sessions`, which every command maps to `DriverError::NoSession` (`driver/mod.rs` spells it `"no such session"`); `commands.rs:432`'s `disconnect` is the ONLY place in the tree that removes an id, so a NoSession always means the frontend forgot first and sent the stale id anyway. Every session opens with `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout=600000` (`driver/postgres/mod.rs:99`), the ten minutes `humanCloseReason` below cites by that line number so the two move together (DESIGN rule 14). No Rust changed this wave.
- 2026-09-16 · **The frontend forgets a tab session in five places, and the connection dot never notices because it tracks the PRIMARY.** `connections.ts`: `markDisconnected`'s tab branch, `connectInner`'s per-profile wipe, `invalidateProfile`, `closeTabSessions`, and `healInner`'s reaping. Each left `useResults.byTab[tab].executedSessionId` — a stamp written only by `run()` — standing, and a dozen call sites handed that stamp to the backend raw: the maintainer's own report (a strip reading `no such session` under a green dot, ⇧⌘R changing nothing) is what a stale stamp on a live-looking connection looks like from the outside.
- 2026-09-16 · **One resolver, `src/stores/liveSession.ts`, replaces edits.ts's commit-only `liveSessionId`.** `liveSessionFor(tabId)` re-derives a tab's live session against `ensureTabSession` and re-stamps `executedSessionId` (on `byTab` and the active mirror, since pg-notice routing keys on the stamp) only when it changed, returning `rebuilt: "tx"` when the dead id held an open transaction (`sessionDiedWithTx`) else `"info"`. `withLiveSession(tabId, fn)` is the one door a backend call goes through: resolve, call; a NoSession race (the id dies between resolve and send) forgets the stale id and retries once before rejecting with the one human message (rule below). `edits.ts`'s `liveSessionId` and `sessionAndSql` are deleted; `RebuiltKind` moved to `liveSession.ts` and `edits.ts` imports it back for `preview.rebuilt`.
- 2026-09-16 · **Session death, however the store learns of it, clears the stamp everywhere it is held — one mechanism, not five edited call sites.** `results.ts` subscribes to `useConnections`: any session id present in the previous `tabSessions` values and absent from the next is nulled out of `executedSessionId` on every tab that named it (`byTab` and the active mirror), with the dying id kept one step longer in a `forgottenStamps` map so a later tx verdict can still be asked. Rows, statements and the profile stamp are untouched — only the dead handle goes.
- 2026-09-16 · **Heal re-stamps the tab it healed and clears only the strips a dead session wrote.** `healInner`'s old eager warm (`ensureTabSession` on the active tab, touching no stamp and no strip — why ⇧⌘R changed nothing on screen) is replaced by a dynamic `import("./liveSession").then(({ afterHeal }) => afterHeal(profileId))` (`connections.ts` stays under results/browser/edits, matching its existing `./tabs`/`./schema` dynamic imports). `afterHeal` re-resolves the active tab's session ONLY when that tab's own result came from the healed profile — resolving a tab that ran elsewhere would connect a profile the user closed, since `ensureTabSession` mints a missing primary (`heal.ts:34`) — then clears death-class strips (`results.clearDeathStrip`, `browser.clearDeathStrips`, `edits.clearDeathStrip`) on every tab of that profile, matched on `isDeathStrip` (the death sentences themselves, stricter than the SQLSTATE-blind `isSessionDeath` results.ts reaps on) rather than on words a real error can share (a unique violation on `connections_pkey`, a missing `connection_log` table, a `reset_at` column all pass `isDeathStrip` as false in the test file).
- 2026-09-16 · **The backend's own words never reach a reader.** `humanSessionError(err)` rewrites a `"no such session"` message to `"connection to this tab was lost. Refresh to reconnect"` at every strip-writing site (`results.ts` globalError, `browser.ts` draftError/countError/paginationBroken, `edits.ts` lastError, `Inspector.tsx`'s full-value error); everything else passes through untouched. `grep -rn "no such session" src` returns only `liveSession.ts` (the constant, the regex, two doc comments) and its own test file — no other module carries the literal.
- 2026-09-16 · **Ten-plus call sites converted from the raw stamp to the resolver, verified against the diff rather than the plan.** `browser.ts` `insertRow` and `loadMore`/`fetchPage` (the identity check in `appendPage` now measures the sid `withLiveSession` actually used, not the one requested); `Grid.tsx`'s delete-rows (the undo offer is stamped with the session that committed) and its FK "Referenced by" fan-out (one `liveSessionFor` for the whole estimate batch, not per-query); `Inspector.tsx` `fetchCell`; `edits.ts` `refetchMap` and `ensureMap` (now gated on `executedSql` alone, read tab-exact rather than off the mirror); `ImportWizard.tsx` (`browseTarget()` returns `{tabId, profileId}`, no liveness hand-check, so Cancel targets whatever session the run is really on). None of these read `executedSessionId` any more.
- 2026-09-16 · **One `anySessionOn(profileId)` in `connections.ts` replaces four copies of "primary or any live tab session"** (`browser.ts` `exactCount`, `StructureTab.tsx` `pickSession`, `TableBrowser.tsx`'s estimate, `FkPicker.tsx`'s `preferredSessionId`, `ServerInfo.tsx`, `DbSwitcher.tsx`) — DESIGN rule 15. `results.ts`'s planner estimate keeps primary-only, on purpose, and is unmoved. - 2026-09-16 · **`grep -rn executedSessionId src` now returns only allowed reads:** `liveSession.ts` (the resolver itself), `results.ts` (the field, `blankTab`, `run`'s write, `cancel`'s target, the rule-2 subscription, the pg-notice router), `browser.ts`'s `appendPage` identity check, and the two test files. ### Agent · E2 (refresh tiers: ⌘R soft, ⇧⌘R hard with the window sweep, 2026-09-16; evidence: the maintainer-approved sketch and the probe at 640/960/1280)

- 2026-09-16 · **The sketch (`qwry-agent-lab/docs/refresh-sketch-e2.html`) is the spec, and `frontReachMs` is its projection verbatim.** `x = cx + cy·0.445·H/W` over `.v2-shell`'s own rect, `p = (x+0.18)/2.16`, clamped to `[.05,.95]`, scaled by the 720ms sweep — at 1280×640 with the frames' own geometry this gives tree 138ms, main 261ms, inspector 375ms, matching the stills' captions exactly (at t=260 only the sidebar cycles; at t=460 sidebar, rows and inspector all do).
- 2026-09-16 · **`track()` resolves R3's two clauses the sketch states but a skeleton cannot honor literally** ("the cycle starts at the front" and "a fetch settling within 150ms never shows a skeleton" — a shown skeleton cannot be un-shown). `showIn = max(frontReach − elapsed, 150ms)`: the fetch fires at t0 untouched, the skeleton waits for the front, never appears sooner than the 150ms grace, and once up holds ≥ `--dur-slow`. A rejected `work` settles the cycle too, so nothing stays blank.
- 2026-09-16 · **`hardRefresh` now awaits heal's verdict**: `requestHeal` returns `Promise<boolean>` (ok && still-armed); every existing caller ignores the value, and `manual=true` is reached only from `hardRefresh`. R6's retry is wired the other direction, `heal.ts` notifying `refresh.ts` (a dynamic import, since `refresh.ts` statically imports `heal.ts`) on every settle: `healSettled` acts only when a failed hard refresh is still standing (`dead` on the store), so the manual verdict is never double-handled, and it backdates `startedAt` by 720ms so every surface cycles at once — a retry is not a gesture and plays no band.
- 2026-09-16 · **`refreshFx` keeps `begin`/`resolve` and gains `autoClap`** (split + clap, no spin, no band) for background heals; `resolve`'s `ok` parameter went with the deleted header shine, since the clap reads the same either way and the verdict lands on `connState`'s own colours — a parameter nothing read would have been a lie.
- 2026-09-16 · **The sweep is CSS, not a WAAPI call**: one keyframe, `calc(var(--dur-slow) * 3)`, linear, on a component keyed by `sweepSeq` and unmounted on `animationend`, so the token duration stays where `design-lint` can see it. `position: fixed` at `z-index 14` rather than absolute inside `.v2-shell`, so no card's stacking context changes; 14 sits above every content layer (grid sticky chrome 10, tx banner 12) and below the first overlay (`fn-search` 15) — this codebase's actual z-index census for "above the cards, below every menu."
- 2026-09-16 · **`refreshActiveTab` is the one door both tiers end at**, R4's guards (staged edits, a last run that wrote) sitting in front of both. A table tab delegates to `useBrowser.refresh()`; a query tab reruns its own `executedSql` with `{refresh: true}` pinned to `executedProfileId`, never the rail selection, so a refresh reads the connection the rows came from.
- 2026-09-16 · **`readOnlyHeads` (`src/lib/sqlHeads.ts`) opens the two heads that lie about themselves**: `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` runs what it explains, and a `WITH` clause can hold an `INSERT`. Both are read with the lexer the tree already had (`headToken`, `parseCtes`), never a regex over the buffer; a comment-prefixed `EXPLAIN` it cannot parse is refused rather than re-run.
- 2026-09-16 · **The status strip follows the PANE, not the fetch** (`busy = running || cycling` in `ResultsPane`): R3 holds a skeleton ≥ `--dur-slow` after the fetch settles, so `refreshing` alone would go false while the bars are still up, and printing `"38.4 ms · just now"` under a skeleton reports a result nobody has seen (LESSONS 13). The refreshing label, the rerun button and the timing now key off `busy`. The `↻` rerun button itself was still bypassing the store (`useResults.run` direct, no tier, no R4 guards — clicking it after an UPDATE would silently run the write a second time); it now calls `useRefresh.getState().softRefresh()`.
- 2026-09-16 · **A refresh note takes the strip alone, exactly as LostLine already does** — `.status-bar` is a fixed 24px box (DESIGN rule 2: states swap content, never resize the strip); rendering the note beside `PendingEditsStatus` wrapped to two lines and repeated "Commit ⌘S" twice (rule 14). The note already names the count and both actions, so the chip beside it was the duplicate (rule 15).
- 2026-09-16 · **`GridSkeleton` mounts only over a real grid** (`!stmt.error && stmt.columns.length > 0`) and returns null when it cannot measure — it was painting an opaque `--bg-panel` rectangle over an `OK · N rows affected` pane and over error panes, a blank, which is the one thing R3 forbids. `runExactCount(recount?)` likewise skips the `exactCount: null` blank when the WHERE has not changed, so a re-count that dies leaves the number that is still true.
- 2026-09-16 · **Selection on swap is kept by index when the row count is unchanged, cleared otherwise** (`Grid.tsx`, keyed on `refreshedAt` rather than `rows.length`, so streaming batches and `loadMore` do not trigger it). The PK re-apply half of R4 lives on the INSPECTOR target instead (`results.ts`'s `reinspectRow` + `pkColsOf`, reading the editability map captured before the run) — that is the row a reader is actually reading, re-aimed while the old rows are still on screen. `executedSessionId`/`executedProfileId` stamp even when a refresh fails, while `statements`/`executedSql`/`executedOffset`/`refreshedAt` move only on the swap — a healed hard tier hands the tab a rebuilt session, and leaving the stamp on the dead one is the E1 bug again. The refresh run's shadow buffer appends in place (`st.rows.push`) rather than rebuilding the array per batch, since it is never rendered and a 50k result arrives in ~100 batches.
- 2026-09-16 · **Test fixtures are now typed off the stores themselves** (`ReturnType<typeof useX.getState>['byTab'][string]`), every `as never` removed — that is how the browse fixture had been setting `{column, op, value}` against a `Filter` of `{col, op, value, enabled, conj}` and the cast made it compile forever while the filter silently never reached the SQL.
- 2026-09-16 · **The tree surface is marked on the SCROLLER**, not `.schema-tree`'s root (`scroll.dataset.refreshSurface` in the virtualizer effect): the list is as tall as the schema, so a 2,000-table snapshot's centre sits far below the window, and the box the band actually crosses is the scroll box. One skeleton bar per VISIBLE virtual row, each wearing that row's own class (`tree-schema` / `tree-table[.nested]` / `tree-col` / …), so every indent, height and gap in the ladder answers for the skeleton too and no second geometry can drift (rule 14); bar width `min(88%, 24 + label.length·3.6)%`, headings capped at 46%. `schema.ts` needed nothing: a failed introspect keeps the old snapshot, so a refetch keeps the old tree standing throughout (R3's "nothing stays blank"), and no new error slot was added for a rare failure on a live connection (rule 12, rule 15) — flagged to the maintainer in Open rather than decided here.
- 2026-09-16 · **The canvas write seam is registered, not imported**: `canvas.ts`'s `setWidgetRefetch` reaches `stores/refresh.ts` through a dynamic import at module end (the same shape as `setCanvasPort`), so the document chunk never statically pulls in the store that reaches every surface in the window. It was fire-and-forget (`void import('./refresh')`) — a ⇧⌘R in the first frames after boot would find the slot empty and silently refetch nothing — and is now an exported `widgetSeam` promise that `refreshCanvas` awaits before reading the slot. `refreshWidget` captures block + meta + session BEFORE the first await, runs one `execute` on `anySessionOn(doc.profileId)`, and lands through `replaceResult`; a failure keeps the old rows and writes the block's own `mismatch` strip. A widget is refetchable only when it is a result with a `sql`, is not standing on a comparison, and `readOnlyHeads(sql)` says the statement is safe to run again — the same verdict a query tab's rerun reads.
- 2026-09-16 · **The canvas fixture was re-cut to the sketch's own arrangement** (2×2 cells, two results and the note across the top row, the third opening the next, `lastColumns: 8` — the count the canvas affords at 1280 with the side pane closed) from an earlier `w:4,h:4`/`lastColumns 12` layout the product's grid engine rendered one-per-viewport. The side pane is CLOSED for `e2-canvas`: nothing is selected there, the sketch gives that card to Ask, and this window has no Ask, so a third of the frame reading only "Select a cell to inspect" would be dead text (rule 12).
- 2026-09-16 · **The inspector skeleton is measured, not described** (`Inspector.tsx`'s `useSkeletonBox`): it was painting a repeating gradient the height of the whole pane against a resting inspector holding a header plus one value card. It now reads the value's own text box in a layout effect (offset, inner width/height, computed line-height) and paints bars only there, at that line rhythm — one line of mono gets one bar, a seven-line JSON tree gets seven.
- 2026-09-16 · **One skeleton language, not four.** Four surfaces had each written an identical `@keyframes X { to { opacity: .55 } }`; `tokens.css` now defines `skel-breathe` once and `--skel-lines` once, parameterised by `--skel-line-h`/`--skel-line-gap`, so a surface sets the knob rather than copying the gradient. Geometry stays each surface's own (that is what R3 is about); only the breath and the bar rhythm are shared. `grid.css`'s reduced-motion comment claimed the tokens already killed its pulse; tokens.css only collapses `animation-duration` to 0.01ms, which on an `infinite alternate` strobes rather than stills — grid now carries the explicit `animation: none` override the tree and canvas already had.
- 2026-09-16 · **The db glyph and the crumb dot now wear the connection.** `DbSwitcher` read `connState` only in its connect guard and rendered no tint, so a dead frame showed a healthy blue glyph beside "connection lost"; `.sb-dbhead` now carries the `connState` class, mapped (`v2.css`) to the rail's own colours (`connecting`→`--warn`, `error`→`--danger`, extended to `disconnected`, which the rail hides but a header on screen because the profile once connected cannot), transitioned over `--dur-slow` since a verdict is not an instant swap.
- 2026-09-16 · **The freeze never decides which surfaces cycle.** A fallback that stamped the harness's `cycling` map from the store's own projection, absent a `__freeze`, was producing false frames (`e2-dead@1500` showed tree, grid and inspector all blanking — the exact picture R6 forbids). `freezeAt` now only calls `useRefresh.__freeze(t)` if it ever exists, `flushSync`s, cancels every scheduled timer/rAF and stubs `setTimeout`/`requestAnimationFrame`, and pauses every running animation where it stands; the store ran the act in real time, and its map at `t` IS the answer. `useRefresh.__freeze` itself stays absent from the store contract — the harness freezes correctly without it and the probe recomputes the projection independently, so a dev-only second clock inside the store would be a second implementation of WHEN.
- 2026-09-16 · **The probe (`docs/research/e2-frames/e2-probe.ts`) reads `cycling` from the store and the class from the DOM, and asserts both**, since the tree's ancestor-mark (a deliberate choice: the scroller is the box a reader sees and the box the front crosses) had made a naive `classList` probe read "tree cycles 0 times" for a tree that cycles fine. Each surface also gets a "skeleton is in the DOM" assertion. R3's 150ms grace runs from the SEND (`tauriShim.ts`'s `window.__harness.e2Calls`, a timestamped log of `introspect`/`execute_stream`/`execute(widget:<id>)`/`session_probe`/`connect`, reset at `data-harness-seeded`), not from the gesture, since the hard tier cannot send anything until heal answers. The probe recomputes the locked projection independently (a second implementation catches a wrong box, element or clamp) and separately asserts against the band's own sampled rectangle that nothing ever blanks before its physical leading edge. All six cases run at 640/960/1280 — 193 assertions, the inspector at 640 a SKIP (the pane is closed there) rather than a silent pass.
- 2026-09-16 · **Final gate: gates fresh, in a clean shell, and the probe re-run rather than trusted from the wave's own log (LESSONS 5/9).** `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean · `bun scripts/design-lint.ts` **0 finding(s)** (census mode) · `bun test` **1,384 pass / 0 fail** (78 files, 66,424 `expect()` calls). The wave's own probe log stood at 185/193; re-run cold against the shipped tree on a fresh `vite --port 1437`, it read **189/193**, and two further `--cases e2-canvas` re-runs converged on the same four failures both times: `e2-canvas · hard @ 1280` — tree and all three widgets show a normal cycle (skeleton up, plausible duration) but the SEND log holds no `introspect`/`execute` call for any of them, only at 1280, never at 640 and only sometimes at 960. Traced to two matching early-returns that resolve their `track()`ed promise without ever calling the backend: `surfacePass` (`refresh.ts:191`, `const sid = sessions[profileId]; if (sid) { … }`, silently skipped when falsy) and `refreshWidget` (`canvas.ts:1830`, `if (!session) return`) — both read `anySessionOn`/`sessions[profileId]` synchronously at a point that appears to race `afterHeal`'s own session registration, worse at wider windows. Not fixed this pass (unverified without the probe's own instrumentation, and outside this seat's mandate to gamble at the finish line); flagged in Open for whoever owns `liveSession.ts`/`afterHeal` next. `localhost:1420` (the maintainer's own dev build) confirmed 200 before and after; no `vite` of this pass left running on 1437. [Superseded 2026-09-17: measured, the cause was the probe's t0 anchor; see the fix-round bullet below.]
- 2026-09-17 · **Fix round before merge: `LostLine` was the one slot still guessing at a fact `useRetrying` already settles.** Root cause: `.status-lost` (`grid.css`) painted `--warn` unconditionally, while the crumb dot and the sidebar glyph (the prior bullet, above) had already grown a `.retrying` class off `useRetrying`, true only while `dead.retryAt` is non-null. The two go dark for the SAME reason `refresh.ts`'s own no-session paths do — `heal.ts`'s `run()` clears `retryAt` the moment it is not `stillHere` (line 70) or never armed to begin with (line 46-48), and `surfacePass`'s no-session branch inherits whichever `nextRetryAt` that leaves — so a heal that gave up outright, not merely a heal still backing off, is exactly the case the strip mis-painted amber. This is the wave's own honesty rule restated for colour, not just for content: no session behind a cycle means no cycle (R3, R6), and no retry behind a countdown means no warn — a strip that keeps its warm colour after the one thing that earned it (a retry actually coming) is gone is the same lie as a skeleton with no fetch behind it. Fix: `LostLine` derives `retrying` from the same `left`/`at` value it already renders (`left !== null`, no second `useRetrying` subscription re-reading the fact from the store — DESIGN rule 14), adds `.retrying` to its own class, and `grid.css` now reads `.status-lost{color:var(--danger)}` / `.status-lost.retrying{color:var(--warn)}`, the identical two-token split `.conn-dot`/`.sb-dbhead` already use. No new token. Probe re-run clean at 411/411 (`docs/research/e2-frames/e2-probe.log`); `e2-dead@1500` re-shot at 640/960/1280 × dark/light.
- 2026-09-17 · **The canvas no-send was the probe's clock, not the store.** Re-checked against an instrumented run rather than trusted from the prior gate's own trace: at `e2-canvas · hard @ 1280`, `sessions` held `{harness-staging: e2-session}` at every point that mattered (`hardRefresh` entry, after `requestHeal`, after `afterHeal`, at `surfacePass`), all three `refreshWidget` calls held a session, and the shim's own log recorded `introspect` and all three widget `execute`s. The four FAIL lines were a timing artifact: `RefreshHarness.tsx` stamps `data-harness-seeded` and fires `hardRefresh` in the same task, the shim answers `session_probe` with no wait, so heal, `afterHeal`, `surfacePass` and the sends land about 13.9ms after the stamp; `e2-probe.ts` anchored t0 on the first 16ms poll tick that OBSERVED the mark, 10.7 to 13.4ms late against a 0.5 to 3ms margin, and when that tick fell after the send burst every send read `t < 0`, so `sentAt()` returned null and a real cycle read as one with nothing behind it. 1280 is the heaviest layout, so its pre-stamp frame is the longest: the failure never showed at 640, only sometimes at 960, always at 1280. Fix: the harness now stamps its own instant (`data-harness-t0 = performance.now()`) and the sampler anchors on that mark, falling back to `performance.now()` only when it is absent; the assertion itself is untouched. Verified under 4x concurrent load: sent times of 26 to 27ms, 32/32 each. Bought along the way, the honesty rule the timing bug had been hiding: `surfacePass` now resolves the tree's session through `connections.anySessionOn` and, with none, sets the R6 dead strip instead of silently tracking a fetch that never fires; `canvas.ts`'s `refreshWidget` with no session throws a `DriverError`-shaped `canvasLost()` ("connection to this canvas was lost. Refresh to reconnect") inside its own try, landing on the block's own mismatch strip instead of returning quietly. Alongside it, `firstLine()` now reads `.message` off any object rather than requiring an `Error` instance: a `DriverError` is a plain object, so every real failed refetch before this fix had been printing `[object Object]` on the strip (LESSONS 9). The lesson for the next harness: it must stamp its own t0, since an observer that only learns of a mark on its next tick will always read some real lateness as a refetch that never went out.

### Agent · E3 (instant loaders: the app answers in the frame, 2026-09-17; evidence: the maintainer's bastion and the probe at 640/960/1280)

- 2026-09-17 · **The maintainer's read confirmed against the tree at b39f432, not assumed.** Every await between ⇧⌘R and the first skeleton, in order: `refresh.ts:179` `await requestHeal(profileId, true)` (the whole bastion round trip, ahead of every surface), `:187` `await afterHeal`, `:189` `await surfacePass`; inside it `:211` `track("tree")` was the first fetch sent at all, then `:213 await refreshActiveTab()`, which itself paid two dynamic imports (`./edits`, `./browser`) before the tab's own fetch; the canvas route added a third (`./canvas`) plus `widgetSeam`. And then `track()` (:104) still waited again: `showIn = max(frontReachMs(el) − since, GRACE_MS)`, so a skeleton blanked only at up to ~684ms (0.95 × the 720ms sweep) plus the 150ms grace — later than the band on any real connection. E2's own harness answered `session_probe` in 0ms, which is why the wave's frames never showed the lag.
- 2026-09-17 · **Rule 1 — the whole plan is decided from store state alone in one synchronous write, before any await.** `hardRefresh` now builds a `Plan` (`surfacePass` → `planTree` + `planActive`, `refreshCanvas` → `planCanvas`) synchronously, writes `tier`/`profileId`/`sweepSeq`/`startedAt`/`cycling`/`note`/`dead` in one `set`, then calls `plan.send()` in the same tick and starts `requestHeal` beside it. Measured: the store's cycling edge lands 0-1ms after t0, the DOM commit at 26-35ms. `requestHeal` (`session_probe`) itself dispatches at 2-3ms in the probe log, ahead of `introspect`/`execute_stream` (27-43ms) — the one place the shipped tree does not literally match "beside them, never ahead of them": `schema.ts`'s `cacheSig` and `results.ts`'s `run` each pay a dynamic import (`./connections`, `./edits`, `./danger`) before their own invoke, a ~25-40ms gap the probe's own log shows plainly. Non-blocking (the loaders are already up regardless of send order) and carried to Open below rather than fixed blind by this seat.
- 2026-09-17 · **Rule 2 — GRACE_MS and frontReachMs are deleted outright, not softened** (DESIGN rule 15), along with `surfaceEl()`, the `SWEEP_MS` projection and `healSettled`'s backdated `startedAt`. `track(surface, work)` (its `el` parameter went with them) now only holds: the skeleton is already up from the plan's write, `MIN_SHOW_MS = 240` runs from the call, and it hides the instant its own fetch lands. `planCanvas` fires widgets in `seam.blocks()` document order.
- 2026-09-17 · **Rule 3 — the dead path is one write**, `set({ cycling: {}, dead: {...} })`; `track`'s `hide` is guarded by `s.cycling[surface] !== true` so a late landing cannot reopen a cycle. Probe evidence at all three widths: dead declared ~11ms after the wire said so, every surface off its loader in that same write, no sample with rows=0, strip up, one band run, heal still retrying after.
- 2026-09-17 · **Rule 4 — the lab's probe (`qwry-agent-lab/docs/research/e2-frames/e2-probe.ts`) was re-cut**: `frontPass`, `projected`, `SWEEP_MS`, `CLAMP_MAX_MS`, `TAN24` and `GRACE_MS` are gone, replaced by `edges` (every store write, timestamped inside a zustand subscription) and `samples` (the DOM every 8ms). Assertions: every refetching surface wears `.cycling` within 2 frames of `data-harness-t0`; the band's first sample is within that same window; a shown skeleton holds ≥ 240ms; the five e2-dead clauses; e2-table-staged/e2-query-wrote never cycle main; soft shows no band with main and inspector cycling together. `tauriShim.ts`'s `session_probe` gained the same `lat` mechanism the fetches use (default 400ms via `E2_LAT.probe`), and `e2-dead` answers dead only after it; `RefreshHarness`'s `freezeAt` now pins the band to `min(t, dur)` rather than always finishing it, so `e2-table@0` is a picture of the band at ITS OWN START.
- 2026-09-17 · **Rule 5 — `refresh.test.ts` reads `hardRefresh`'s state with zero microtasks run**: `const act = hardRefresh('p1')` then a synchronous `getState()` shows `cycling` already true and `sweepSeq` already bumped; the heal mock answers on a 40ms delay while recording `cyclingAtHeal`, making "the probe went out beside them, never ahead of them" an assertion. All of rule 5's cases are present (a landing at 50ms still shows ≥240ms; a dead probe after loaders returns them and sets `dead`; the staged guard sets the note in the same write and never cycles main; soft shows no `sweepSeq` bump and cycles main at once). - 2026-09-17 · **Rule 6 — 48 stills, both themes, 640/960/1280**, copied to `qwry-agent-lab/docs/research/e3-frames/`: `e2-table@0` (all loaders up, band at its own start), `@260`, `@1000`, `e2-dead@200` (loaders up, probe pending), `e2-dead@1500` (old rows back, amber, retrying), `e2-canvas@100` (all three widgets cycling together), `e2-table-staged@100`, and the soft tier's `e2-table@50` (no band, main and inspector on loaders).

### Agent · E4 (the conversational contract: the answer is what the model said, 2026-09-17; evidence: the maintainer's canvas thread and the Pagila row)

- 2026-09-17 · **The diagnosis, read from the app's own stored thread, not assumed.** The maintainer's canvas thread's fourth exchange ("what what can you create on canvas") was informational and asked for no data; Haiku 4.5 answered in prose and called no tool. `extractSql`'s `raw` branch (loop.ts, pre-wave) took that prose as the exchange's SQL anyway, ran it itself as a loop-invented `final-N` step, hit the AST gate's `this is prose, not SQL` refusal, fed `repairMessage` back, and looped to the cap — the W7 circuit breaker never fired because it only counts the model's OWN `run_sql` calls, and this exchange made none. The exchange stored `failed` with the prose in the `sql` column, so the failure block offered `Fix It` over prose, and a `Fix It` click re-sent the prose to get `[No response - system error loop detected…]` back, also filed as `sql`. The two texts (question and prose) are copied nowhere but `loop.test.ts` (byte-equal, sha256-checked against the stored turn), `agent-prose.test.ts` (a prefix) and `context.test.ts` (the question).
- 2026-09-17 · **"Wants data" is three code facts, never the prefilter's picked list.** `wantsData = mustIncludeFor(meta, mentions).length > 0 || isRisky(question) || tablesNamed(question, meta, synonyms).length > 0` (loop.ts, computed once before turn 1). `candidates()` appends the top-3 FK hub tables and a one-hop expansion to every question, so `picked.length > 0` is never empty on any schema holding a foreign key — the maintainer's own question picks tables on Pagila and on his DB alike (`context.test`: `candidates(q).length > 0` beside `tablesNamed(q) == []`). It is also not a column hit: on Pagila the maintainer's question scores `customer` through its `create_date` column alone, which would have nudged his exact question were a column hit enough. `tablesNamed` (new export, `context.ts`) is the `named` axis of the prefilter's own lexical scoring (a token of the table's name or base name, 3x/+6, or a synonym pin) — one derivation, not a second guess; recall held 28/28 → later 31/33 unchanged.
- 2026-09-17 · **The nudge is one user turn in the post step, never a run.** When the model stops with no tool call and the composed answer carries no fence (`fenced()`, `extractSql` minus its `raw`/`none` branches) and the loop holds no run of the model's own (`lastHeld()` null): if `wantsData && !ranStatement && !nudged && turns < maxTurns`, push a `nudge` trace step (`ms: 0`, since the step did no work and printing `0 ms` would be a lie) and `nudgeMessage()` (`prompt.ts`, beside `repairMessage`, never in the cached `SYSTEM_PROMPT` — `PROMPT_VERSION` stays v4) as a user turn, and continue. `ranStatement` is set off the model's own tool calls (`run_sql`, `canvas_write`, `canvas_replace`) on both the driven and the `ownsLoop` path. Exactly one nudge per exchange; it counts as a turn; on the cap's last turn the model's words are the answer rather than a nudge that could only end `turn_cap`.
- 2026-09-17 · **A closing fence gets exactly three outcomes, never a fourth.** A fence naming a run the loop already holds (`heldFor`, `sameSql`, latest match) is adopted with no re-run. Otherwise the loop runs it once, trace id `closing-fence` (exported `CLOSING_FENCE` from `loop.ts`): rows → answered with that sql and run; the gate refuses it as prose (`isProseRefusal`) → answered in words, sql and run both null, no repair, no error event, the refused step stays in the trace (the trace tells the truth even about a step that failed); any other error → one `repairMessage` if `!repaired && turns < maxTurns`, else `failed` carrying that sql (the only way a `failed` verdict carries `sql` now). No fence and a held run → answered under that run's own statement, R1's "last successful run" reading.
- 2026-09-17 · **Six places the old loop-invented final run had leaked assumptions, each named and replaced**: the trace id `final-${turns}` → `CLOSING_FENCE`; `runs.last` (one slot, overwritten) → `held[]` (every run the model made, read by `heldFor`/`lastHeld`, which `turn_cap` and `cancelled` verdicts now read); the repair re-send guarded only by `turns < maxTurns` (up to the cap) → once per exchange (`repaired`); `extractSql`'s `raw` branch reaching the hybrid post step → `fenced()` never returns it; two loop tests that had asserted the loop's OWN invented run (`'their tool results are recorded, never re-executed'`, whose one `runSql` call is now the closing-fence fetch, trace ids pinned `['a', CLOSING_FENCE]`; `'a canvas_write reaches the tool'`, which had asserted 1 `runSql` — the loop running the model's own prose as SQL — now asserts 0, verdict pinned `answered`/null).
- 2026-09-17 · **`claude -p`'s own loop never surfaces `run_sql` rows to TS** (the child answers the MCP call in Rust; the adapter yields text only), so `held` is empty on that path and a closing fence is fetched once even when the child ran the identical statement — the grid's only source of rows on the app's default provider, both before and after this wave. A fence-less prose answer after a child run stays answered with `sql` null and no grid: the model did not close with a statement, and re-running its last exploratory call would be a guess the contract does not make.
- 2026-09-17 · **The store's one invented fact, found and removed.** `openThread` read `agent_answers.sql` as the exchange's statement without asking what it was, and the old loop had written the model's own prose into that column — which is how the maintainer's reopened thread reads `query failed` with a paragraph in the SQL field and `Fix It` over it. Both readers (`openThread`, `replayPairs`) now go through one function, `storedSql(turn, answer)` (`src/stores/agent.ts`): the answer row's sql, else the model's text but only when it fenced (E4's own R2, applied retroactively to old rows), kept only when `opensLikeStatement` says it opens like one. `opensLikeStatement` mirrors the gate's own `opens_like_sql` / `SQL_LEADS` (`src-tauri/src/agent.rs`) on the TS side — a live run asks the gate, a row read back out of appdb has no gate to ask, so the question is asked in the store, with the source named in a comment. `errorFromStatus` needed no new branch: with `storedSql` upstream, a non-null `sql` IS a statement, so `kind: "sql"` and the `Fix It` that rides it can only appear for a statement that actually failed.
- 2026-09-17 · **`FailureBlock` and `AnswerBlock` needed no runtime guard for R4.** `FailureBlock` already showed `Fix It` only on `sql !== null`; `AnswerBlock` already omitted the result block, status line and failure block when sql, run and preview are all null. Both gained a header paragraph stating the contract they are already written against, instead of a third copy of the predicate. One real surface defect this exposed: `.ans-strip` was a fixed 24px row reserved even when a prose answer ran no chip-producing work — true of every reloaded thread since W1 (appdb never kept chips). `AnswerBlock` now mounts the strip only while the exchange streams or holds a chip; the streaming case is untouched.

The subscription is taken beside the canvas `bridge` const, before any child exists, released in the same `finally` (`runBridge?.()`), so finish, turn cap, failure and cancel all let go; the bridge hands every run to every reader, and the LOOP is the single gate, dropping any run whose `sessionId` is not its own before pushing `{ sql, run }` into `held[]` in arrival order — the identical shape the driven path already filled, so `heldFor`/`lastHeld` and E4's R1 read one list on both paths with no change to either.
